### DEBT-BUILD-COMPOSITE-TSC-PARALLEL-001 — composite tsc project-reference builds can race under nx parallelism on a cold cache (transient TS2307)

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-21, full `nx release --dry-run` (build/dist-versioning-plugin). `agent-engine-orchestrator:build` failed `TS2307: Cannot find module '@adhd/agent-store-prompts'` during the parallel publish-phase build — yet the **same build passes in isolation** (`npx nx build agent-engine-orchestrator` → EXIT 0) and after a full ordered `npx nx run-many -t build` (→ EXIT 0, all 54 projects). So it is a build-ORDER race, not a real resolution break (the tsconfig path `@adhd/agent-store-prompts → src/index.ts`, the package name, and the import specifier all match).
**Where:** agent tsc packages build with `tsconfig.lib.json` `"composite": true` project references; under high nx parallelism on a cold cache a consumer's `tsc` can begin before a referenced project's declaration output is visible. Same composite-tsc family as the external-`tsc --build` stray-`.js`/`TS6307` incident recorded later in this file.
**Impact:** a cold-cache one-shot `nx release` (no build-first) can transiently fail. **Mitigated:** the `pnpm release` script is `pnpm run build && pnpm exec nx release` — building first (proper dependency order, warm cache) sidesteps the race; the publish phase then gets cache hits.
**Fix direction:** `composite:true` is NOT trivially removable — each package's root `tsconfig.json` declares `references:[{path:"./tsconfig.lib.json"}]`, and TS requires a referenced project to be composite, so dropping it breaks the project-reference topology + `tsc -b` + IDE typecheck (verified 2026-07-21). Options that remain: (a) add explicit cross-package `references` from each consumer's `tsconfig.lib.json` to its `@adhd/*` deps' tsconfigs so `tsc` itself enforces build order (larger topology change); (b) constrain the tsc build's nx parallelism; (c) keep the build-first mitigation as the accepted answer. **Blocked on a deterministic reproduction:** the race is intermittent (warm/isolated builds always pass), so per the repo verification standard no fix can be teeth-proven until it can be reliably reproduced — a speculative edit here would be unprovable and risk regressing 6 packages. Do NOT change composite without a repro.
**Citations:** [build/dist-versioning-plugin, claude (opus), 1: `agent-engine-orchestrator:build` TS2307 in the parallel release dry-run; 2: terminal `npx nx build agent-engine-orchestrator` → EXIT 0 (isolated); 3: terminal `npx nx run-many -t build` → EXIT 0 (54 projects, ordered); 4: packages/agent/agent-engine-orchestrator/tsconfig.json (`references:[{path:./tsconfig.lib.json}]`) + tsconfig.lib.json (`composite:true`) — composite is load-bearing for the reference]
**Status:** OPEN — non-blocking (build-first mitigates); real fix blocked on a deterministic repro (see fix direction). 6 packages carry `composite:true`.

> **DEBT-DISPATCH-CLI-TEST-DECL-BLOAT-001 — RESOLVED 2026-07-21.** Excluded `src/test/**` from
> dispatch-cli's lib build (`tsconfig.lib.json` + vite-plugin-dts). Test decls no longer ship;
> API types (index/api/lib `.d.ts`) intact; gates + 30 tests green. See CHANGELOG.md.

### BUG-YARN-LOCKFILE-MISMATCH-001 — `packageManager` pins Yarn Berry 4.5.3 but `yarn.lock` is Classic v1 format

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-17, researching `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` (worktree dependency-setup strategy).
**Where:** `package.json:143` pins `"packageManager": "yarn@4.5.3+sha512..."` (Yarn Berry). `yarn.lock:1-2` reads `# THIS IS AN AUTOGENERATED FILE. DO NOT EDIT THIS FILE DIRECTLY. # yarn lockfile v1` — Classic format, currently 19,679 lines.
**Impact:** Berry does not read Classic-format lockfiles. Any bare `yarn install` under the pinned binary silently discards the lockfile and performs a full unanchored re-resolution (observed growing the file to 27,245 lines in a live incident before being caught and reverted — see `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §1.1). This is a live, standing defect independent of any package-manager migration decision.
**Fix direction:** either (a) regenerate `yarn.lock` in Berry format under the pinned 4.5.3 binary and verify `nx build`/`nx test` still resolve cleanly, or (b) if the pnpm migration proposed in `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.1 is adopted, this becomes moot — retire `yarn.lock` entirely in favor of `pnpm-lock.yaml`. Either way, do not leave the current mismatched pair in place.
**DECISION 2026-07-20 (owner: pseudosky):** pursue option (b) — **migrate the repo to pnpm**, done SAFELY and SEPARATELY (not blocking in-flight work). Evidence that yarn is the *current* canonical PM (so this is a real migration, not a cleanup): `packageManager: yarn@4.5.3` + both CI workflows run `corepack enable`; `yarn.lock` current (2026-07-09), `pnpm-lock.yaml` stale (2025-09-24), `package-lock.json` cruft (`DEBT-PACKAGE-LOCK-STRAY-001`). This lockfile shadowing is also the **root cause of `BUG-REPO-PRECOMMIT-DEPCHECK-STRIPS-USED-DEPS-001`** (Nx parses the stale/mismatched `yarn.lock` and, in a `node_modules`-less worktree, mis-resolves deps → strips them). Migration scope: switch `packageManager` to pnpm, add `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, regenerate `pnpm-lock.yaml`, retire `yarn.lock` + `package-lock.json`, update CI corepack/install, and adopt the per-worktree near-zero-cost `node_modules` setup (pnpm global virtual store) from `worktree-workflow.md` §3.1 — which also structurally closes the dep-strip. Use `~/dev/ai/scratch/pnpm-migrate` (refuses a dirty tree) per global tooling guidance. **Non-blocking** — the dep-strip is already mitigated on main (verify-only hook + node_modules guard, commit `8e6fd54b`).
**Status:** OPEN — deferred by decision to a safe, separate pnpm migration (see DECISION above).

### DEBT-PACKAGE-LOCK-STRAY-001 — a third, unused `package-lock.json` (npm format) is tracked in git alongside `yarn.lock`

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-17, same research pass.
**Where:** `/Users/nix/dev/node/adhd/package-lock.json` (1.5MB, npm-lockfile-format, tracked). `git log -1 -- package-lock.json` → commit `b1580fd6`, message `chore(repo): commit pre-existing working-tree state (not authored this session)` (2026-07-09) — i.e. it was swept in incidentally, not deliberately maintained. `.gitignore` has no `package-lock.json` entry, so it stays tracked and can silently drift.
**Impact:** a third lockfile format tracked alongside `yarn.lock` (Classic-format, see `BUG-YARN-LOCKFILE-MISMATCH-001`) compounds package-manager confusion — any tool or agent that runs `npm install` instead of `yarn install` would resolve against this stale, uncoordinated lockfile.
**Fix direction:** `git rm package-lock.json` once the package-manager decision in `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.1 is made (pnpm, or a corrected `yarn.lock`) — only one lockfile should remain, matching whichever manager is chosen. Human-approved removal per `AGENTS.md`'s file-removal rules.
**Status:** OPEN.

### DEBT-BUILD-VERSION-SYNCDEPS-REDUNDANT-001 — `sync-deps` (`@nx/dependency-checks` via ESLint) is uncached and re-invoked 2-3x per project per release, dominating `version`'s wall-clock

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-23, BUILD-TOOLING-METRICS-001 (centralized `tools/nx-plugins/lib/metrics.js` performance-metrics facility) profiling of the `version` task's subprocess overhead, following up on `published-state.json`'s zero-network cache work (which cut `version` from ~74s to ~51s but left it dominated by non-network overhead per that work's own follow-up note).
**Where:** `nx.json` `targetDefaults`: `build` → `dependsOn:["^build","lint"]`, `lint` → `dependsOn:["sync-deps"]`. `sync-deps`/`sync-deps-check` (`tools/nx-plugins/deps/executors/{sync,check}/impl.js`) have **no `cache` setting at all** (neither in `tools/nx-plugins/deps/plugin.js` nor `nx.json`), so every time `sync-deps` is part of a requested task graph it unconditionally re-executes for real — Nx's caching only ever SKIPS a task that is itself configured cacheable; it never infers "this ran moments ago in a sibling invocation." Separately, `version`'s own `dependsOn:["build","assets","^version"]` (`tools/nx-plugins/build/plugin.js`) means `version` ALSO pulls in `build`'s full dependency chain (`^build`→`lint`→`sync-deps`) on every invocation, AND `version/impl.js`'s own `reconcileOwnInternalRanges` step calls `sync-deps`/`sync-deps-check` a SECOND time in-process (to reconcile internal `@adhd/*` ranges) — reusing the exact same executor, which runs the FULL `@nx/dependency-checks` rule (missing/obsolete/version-mismatch, not just internal ranges) both times.
**Measured (real `pnpm release:dry`-equivalent run, all 61 projects, `CI=true ADHD_NX_VERSION_DRY_RUN=1 npx nx run-many -t version --dryRun`, metrics.json from `tools/nx-plugins/lib/metrics.js`):** a single `nx run-many -t version` invocation triggers `sync-deps` (via the build→lint chain) **54 times** (37.6s cumulative real work) **and** `sync-deps-check` (via `version`'s own reconciliation) **54 more times** (38.3s cumulative) — ~76s of ESLint dependency-check work per `version` run alone, BEFORE counting the SEPARATE `sync-deps` pass `pnpm run build` already paid for moments earlier in the same `pnpm release`/`pnpm release:dry` pipeline (confirmed: a subsequent `publish --dryRun` run's dependency chain re-triggered `sync-deps` yet again for a subset of projects). Isolated micro-benchmark: each real `eslint-check.mjs` invocation costs ~550-580ms, of which the actual `execFileSync(eslint, ...)` (loading ESLint's rule engine + `@nx/eslint-plugin`/`@typescript-eslint/*` + resolving `.eslintrc.json` to check ONE `package.json`) is ~96% of that — i.e. this is NOT wasted subprocess-boot overhead (already eliminated, see CHANGELOG BUILD-TOOLING-METRICS-001), it's the inherent cost of the ESLint CLI engine paid 2-3x redundantly per project per release.
**Why not fixed in BUILD-TOOLING-METRICS-001:** two real fix directions exist — (a) make `sync-deps`/`sync-deps-check` genuinely `cache:true` Nx targets (deterministic given package.json + the Nx project graph, a textbook caching candidate) so the 2nd/3rd redundant invocation per release becomes a free cache hit; or (b) have `version`'s `reconcileOwnInternalRanges` skip its own explicit sync-deps/check call when the build-phase `lint`→`sync-deps` pass already ran against unchanged inputs in the same pipeline. Both touch the **`@nx/dependency-checks`/`sync-deps` subsystem**, which has ALREADY shipped one severe, hard-to-detect regression in this exact area (`BUG-REPO-PRECOMMIT-DEPCHECK-STRIPS-USED-DEPS-001` — real, statically-imported deps silently stripped from `package.json`) and is guarded by extensive, hard-won defensive logic (`tools/nx-plugins/deps/eslint-check.mjs`'s `isRealInstall` guard). A caching or dependency-graph change here has repo-WIDE blast radius (every project's `build`/`lint`, not just `version`) and needs its own dedicated verification pass (real installs, worktrees, CI), not a rushed addition to an unrelated profiling task.
**Fix direction:** prototype (a) first — declare `sync-deps`/`sync-deps-check` `cache:true` with `inputs` covering the project's own `package.json` + the Nx project graph's dependency-version hash (NOT the full `production` fileset, to avoid false invalidation), and prove: (1) a real dependency-drift case is still caught after a cache hit is cleared by a real input change, (2) the `--fix` (write) mode's cache semantics don't create a self-referential-input problem (package.json is both input and output when `--fix` mutates it). If (a) proves unsound, fall back to (b) with a `client_request_id`-style provenance flag so `version`'s call only runs when nothing already validated this project's deps in the current pipeline invocation.
**Citations:** [BUILD-TOOLING-METRICS-001, claude (sonnet), 1: nx.json targetDefaults (build/lint dependsOn); 2: tools/nx-plugins/deps/plugin.js (no cache config); 3: tools/nx-plugins/build/plugin.js:62 (version dependsOn); 4: tools/nx-plugins/build/executors/version/impl.js reconcileOwnInternalRanges; 5: live metrics.json from `nx run-many -t version --dryRun` across 61 projects, 2026-07-23; 6: isolated micro-benchmark, node -e spawnSync/import timing of tools/nx-plugins/deps/eslint-check.mjs against packages/agent/agent-base-types/package.json]
**Status:** OPEN — non-blocking (correctness unaffected; this is a pure performance/redundant-work finding). Real numbers above should anchor whoever picks this up; do not re-guess.

### DEBT-NX-WORKTREE-CACHE-001 — Nx worktree-aware build caching is unavailable at this repo's pinned Nx 18.3.4

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-17, same research pass.
**Where:** `node_modules/nx/package.json` → `18.3.4`. Nx's own blog (`nx.dev/blog/nx-22-7-release`) documents worktree-aware task caching and task sandboxing landing at Nx 22.6+ — sharing the `dist`/build-output cache across git worktrees instead of duplicating it per worktree, explicitly framed as valuable for parallel agentic development.
**Impact:** every worktree in this repo currently rebuilds/retests independently with no cross-worktree build-cache sharing, even though this repo runs agents in parallel worktrees as standard practice. This is a real, complementary win to the dependency-setup fix in `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.1 (which addresses `node_modules`/install cost, not build/task cache) but is a separate, larger initiative (4-major-version Nx upgrade) with its own risk surface.
**Fix direction:** track as an independent Nx-upgrade initiative — do not bundle into the worktree dependency-setup work. Re-evaluate once a dedicated Nx-upgrade plan exists.
**Status:** OPEN.

### DEBT-WORKTREE-MERGE-CHECK-001 — deterministic merge-back playbook not implemented; should be a global capability, not repo-local

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-17, `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.2 (design proposed during worktree-workflow research, triggered by a live incident merging a usage-accounting worktree this session).
**Where:** design only, nothing implemented. `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.2 specifies `scripts/worktree-merge-check.sh`: `git merge-tree --write-tree` as a free, zero-mutation first gate (no cleanup needed even on failure), then — only if clean — an ephemeral scratch-worktree merge + scoped `nx affected -t build test`, self-cleaning unconditionally, emitting one exit-code-coded verdict (0=safe, 1=merge-tree conflicts, 2=merges clean but build/test fails, 3=internal error).
**Impact:** agents merging a worktree branch back to main currently improvise multi-turn bash with no single authoritative signal for "is this safe" — burns tokens, produces uncertain and inconsistent outcomes. Observed directly this session.
**Scope note (2026-07-17):** this should NOT be built as a repo-local script only. The underlying problem — an agent needs one deterministic, low-cost signal for "is my worktree branch safe to merge into main" — applies to any repo an agent works in, not just this one. The design in §3.2.1-3.2.2 is already repo-agnostic (`git merge-tree`, an ephemeral scratch worktree, an exit-code verdict); the only repo-specific piece is which build/test command to scope-run. Prefer building this as a globally-available Claude Code capability/skill, with `scripts/worktree-merge-check.sh` here as, at most, a thin repo-specific wrapper around that global capability — not a one-off reimplementation.
**Fix direction:** see `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.2 for the full design. TBD: implement — either as a global skill/capability first with a thin repo wrapper, or as a repo script with an explicit note to generalize later. Open question already flagged in the design doc §4: whether a PASS verdict alone authorizes an unsupervised merge, or every merge still needs human review regardless.
**Status:** OPEN — TBD, design only.

### DEBT-WORKTREE-DIRTY-MAIN-MERGE-001 — no tooling/documented playbook for merging onto a dirty main without stash/new branch; should be a global capability, not repo-local

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-17, `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.3 (same research pass).
**Where:** design only, nothing implemented. `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.3 documents the git mechanics precisely (`git read-tree`/`git merge`'s per-file overwrite-safety check, sourced from git's own docs) and a decision tree: **Case A** — the incoming branch and the uncommitted local changes touch disjoint files — needs no new tooling at all, plain `git merge` already leaves uncommitted files untouched and merges everything else normally today. **Case B** — genuine file overlap — needs, per overlapping file, a `git restore <single-file>` (the explicitly repo-sanctioned single-file exception to the no-whole-tree-restore rule in `AGENTS.md`) followed by `git merge-file` three-way reconciliation, with an honest, disclosed stop for same-line conflicts that cannot be safely automated.
**Impact:** this repo bans `git stash` outright (a prior incident corrupted the Nx project graph) and bans throwaway branches for this purpose, but has no documented/tooled playbook for the resulting case — agents hit a dirty main, don't know the merge is often already safe as-is, improvise, burn tokens, and quit. Observed directly this session.
**Scope note (2026-07-17):** same as `DEBT-WORKTREE-MERGE-CHECK-001` — the git mechanics here (§3.3.1-3.3.2) are entirely generic, not repo-specific. This should be documented/tooled as a globally-available Claude Code playbook/skill ("how to merge onto a dirty working tree without stash or a throwaway branch"), not reinvented per repo.
**Fix direction:** see `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.3 for the full decision tree and exact git commands per case. TBD: (a) at minimum, document Case A as a standing rule agents can rely on without any new tooling — it's a documented fact about `git merge`'s existing behavior, zero code needed; (b) build Case B's automation (scratch-copy + single-file restore + `git merge-file` loop) as lower priority, tracked separately, with an explicit "stop and ask a human" fallback for same-line conflicts until built.
**Status:** OPEN — TBD, design only (Case A is documentation-only and implementable today with zero new code).

### INVESTIGATION-WORKTREE-PNPM-MIGRATION-001 — pnpm migration for worktree deps: spike complete with real numbers, next steps defined, migration NOT started

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-17, follow-up to `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.1 (design doc) — this item tracks the investigation's findings and the concrete next steps for the actual migration, kept separate from the design doc since the doc predates empirical verification and this entry reflects the corrected numbers.
**Where:** `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §2.1.1 (methodology + real numbers) and §3.1 (revised recommendation). Live benchmark run 2026-07-17 against this repo's actual root `package.json` (2,461 resolved packages incl. `better-sqlite3`), fully outside the repo tree to avoid corepack inheriting the pinned `packageManager`.
**Investigation findings (verified, not vendor claims):**
- Cold install (empty store, empty `node_modules`): **31.7s** real wall-clock.
- Second worktree against the now-warm store: **18.6s** real wall-clock — ~40% faster than cold, but **not** "nearly instant" as pnpm's own docs frame it. This is a real, repeatable cost per new worktree even with the store fully warm.
- **Gotcha #1:** pnpm v10 silently blocks dependency lifecycle/build scripts by default (exit 0, warning only) — the first benchmark run looked artificially fast because `better-sqlite3`, `nx`, `@swc/core`, `esbuild`, `cypress`, `@parcel/watcher`, `core-js`, and `unrs-resolver` all had their install scripts skipped, which would leave a non-functional install (no compiled `better-sqlite3` binding). Fix: `pnpm.onlyBuiltDependencies` must be set in `package.json` **before** the first install against a fresh lockfile — adding it after does not retroactively apply (`pnpm install` short-circuits on "Lockfile is up to date, resolution step is skipped").
- **Gotcha #2:** corepack enforces a repo's pinned `packageManager` field on any nested subdirectory, even a scratch dir with its own independent `package.json` lacking that field — any future benchmark/spike must live fully outside this repo's directory tree (confirmed: `/tmp/...`, not `<repo>/tmp/...`).
- Both gotchas + the corrected numbers are logged to memory (`memory-server`, `project_path: /Users/nix/dev/node/adhd`, tagged `pnpm`/`worktree-workflow`/`gotcha`) so they aren't re-discovered from scratch.
**Impact:** the original design's "0 consumed time for unchanged deps" goal is **not** delivered by pnpm's install path alone — 18.6s per new worktree, warm store, is real and would compound across a typical multi-agent session spinning up several worktrees.
**Next steps for the actual build (none started yet):**
1. Confirm the package-manager migration itself with the repo owner — human approval required per `AGENTS.md`'s "you always get human approval before installing external tools" (this is a repo-wide package-manager change: retiring `yarn.lock` + the stray `package-lock.json`, adding `pnpm-workspace.yaml`).
2. Add `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (`packages: ['packages/*/*', 'entrypoint/*']`, `enableGlobalVirtualStore: true`) and set `package.json`'s `packageManager` to a pinned `pnpm@<version>`.
3. Set `pnpm.onlyBuiltDependencies` in `package.json` covering at minimum `better-sqlite3`, `nx`, `@swc/core`, `esbuild`, `cypress`, `@parcel/watcher`, `core-js`, `unrs-resolver` — **before** the first real install, per Gotcha #1 above.
4. Build `scripts/worktree-setup.sh`: hash `package.json`+`pnpm-lock.yaml`; if unchanged from the main checkout's last known-good hash, **skip `pnpm install` entirely** and copy/hardlink an already-built sibling worktree's `node_modules` directly (this is the actual near-zero-cost path — not `pnpm install` itself, which is measurably ~18-32s even warm); if changed, run a real `pnpm install` and update the stored hash.
5. Wire a `WorktreeCreate` hook (`.claude/settings.json`) to invoke the setup script and unify the `.worktrees/` vs `.claude/worktrees/` location split (see `docs/contributing/conventions/worktree-workflow.md` §3.1.5).
6. Re-verify `@adhd/*` runtime resolution against the existing bug at `BACKLOG.md`'s workspace-links entry (`BACKLOG.md:55-58` describes `node_modules/@adhd/` being empty at runtime today with no `workspaces` field) — adding `pnpm-workspace.yaml` may fix or interact with that bug; check both ways before assuming either.
7. Any benchmark/spike work for steps above must run fully outside this repo's directory tree (Gotcha #2).
**Status:** OPEN — investigation complete, migration not started, blocked on human approval (step 1). — dispatch-orchestrator does NOT execute tool calls (headline capability is a stub)
**Discovered:** 2026-07-16, code-reality audit of the dispatch subsystem (import graph + build/test exit codes).
**Where:** `packages/dispatch/dispatch-orchestrator/src/lib/orchestrator.ts:662-671` — for a unit whose operations have no generative content, the loop emits "tool-call execution (@adhd/dispatch-tools) is not wired into dispatch-orchestrator's minimal loop; marking skipped" and proceeds. `@adhd/dispatch-tools` does not exist as a package.
**Impact:** the dispatcher orchestrates/optimizes/serializes and its CLI runs (builds EXIT=0, dispatch-cli 30 tests EXIT=0), but it does not actually EXECUTE tool calls — the core point of a dispatcher. "Shipped/173 tests" claims obscure that the center is hollow.
**Fix direction:** wire real tool-call execution (build the missing `dispatch-tools` or integrate an existing executor); prove it with a demo that drives the real CLI/orchestrator and asserts a tool actually ran. Tracked into `docs/plan/dispatch-completion`.
**Status:** OPEN.

### BUG-APIGEN-GENERATOR-STALE-LOCALBIN-001 — `apigen-generator-nx`'s `generate` executor hardcodes the pre-migration repo-root dist path, so the in-source-dist move makes it silently fall back to the STALE PUBLISHED `npm` package instead of the local, fixed build

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-21, debugging session diagnosing `dispatch-cli`'s `cli-smoke.spec.ts` pre-existing 3 failures (`dist/entrypoint/dispatch-cli/cli/cli.ts:5:20: ERROR: Expected "from" but found "-"`) while fixing `BUG-DISPATCH-SERIALIZER-EXTERNAL-001` (see CHANGELOG.md).
**Where:** `packages/apigen/apigen-generator-nx/src/executors/generate/executor.ts:42` — `const localBin = path.join(context.root, 'dist/entrypoint/apigen-cli/index.js');` then `existsSync(localBin) ? ['node', [localBin, ...cliArgs]] : ['npx', ['@adhd/apigen-cli', ...cliArgs]]`.
**Root cause, fully confirmed:**
1. `dist/entrypoint/apigen-cli/index.js` (repo-root) is the OLD, pre-in-source-dist-migration build location. Post-migration, `apigen-cli`'s own `build` target now writes to `entrypoint/apigen-cli/dist/index.js` (in-source) — the repo-root path is never populated by any current build target. Confirmed: `ls dist/entrypoint/apigen-cli/index.js` → no such file; `entrypoint/apigen-cli/dist/index.js` exists and is fresh.
2. `existsSync(localBin)` is therefore always `false` now, so every `nx run <project>:generate-cli` (dispatch-cli's own target, and any other package using this executor — `entrypoint/environment-cli`, apigen's own generated CLIs, etc.) silently falls through to `npx @adhd/apigen-cli generate ...`.
3. `npx @adhd/apigen-cli` does not find a local bin either (`node_modules/.bin/apigen-cli` does not exist — confirmed via `ls`/`readlink`), so npx fetches/runs the **published npm package** `@adhd/apigen-cli@0.1.0` from the registry (confirmed: `npx @adhd/apigen-cli --version` → `0.1.0`, silent auto-install, no local resolution).
4. That published 0.1.0 build predates whatever fix landed in-repo for `packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-cli-output/src/lib/generate.ts` (`sanitizeIdentifier(pkg.id)` is called at both `_ns`/`_fns` emission sites in current source — verified by reading the file directly, `naming.ts:431-435`'s `sanitizeIdentifier('dispatch-cli')` correctly returns `'dispatch_cli'`) and the `@adhd/apigen-runtime` → `@adhd/apigen-engine-runtime` import-path rename (current source imports the latter; the generated output imports the former). The stale published binary emits the unsanitized, invalid-JS `import * as dispatch-cli_ns from '...'` (hyphen in identifier — not valid JS/TS), which is exactly the `esbuild`/`tsx` parse error (`Expected "from" but found "-"`) `cli-smoke.spec.ts` observes.
**Reproduced directly:** rebuilt `apigen-plugin-cli-output` fresh (`npx nx build apigen-plugin-cli-output`, confirmed a real rebuild via `_ns`/`sanitizeIdentifier` grep against the fresh `dist/index.js`), deleted the stale generated artifact, reran `npx nx run dispatch-cli:generate-cli` — **still emitted the broken, unsanitized `dispatch-cli_ns`**, proving the executor never touches the freshly-built local code at all; it's fetching the stale published package every time, unconditionally, post-migration.
**Impact:** this is the actual, confirmed cause of `dispatch-cli`'s 3 `cli-smoke.spec.ts` failures (previously described only as "a pre-existing apigen-generated-CLI syntax error" against a fix — `packages/apigen/BACKLOG.md`'s `BUG-APIGEN-CLI-001` — that IS fixed in current source but never reaches the generated artifact because of this executor path bug). Same failure mode will hit every other consumer of `@adhd/apigen-generator-nx:generate` post-migration (any package with a `generate-cli`/`generate-mcp`/etc. target), silently shipping stale-generator output with no error (only a parse failure if the staleness happens to produce invalid syntax — a semantic-only staleness would ship silently wrong output with zero signal).
**Second occurrence, confirmed 2026-07-21 (same root, different consumer — `apigen-cli`'s OWN e2e suite):** `entrypoint/apigen-cli/src/test/e2e/{bug-apigen-033-anonymous-default-dispatch,import-source-cjs-format,real-consumer}.spec.ts` each hardcode the identical stale `BUILT_BIN = path.join(REPO_ROOT, 'dist', 'entrypoint', 'apigen-cli', 'index.js')` (repo-root path, never populated post-migration — the real build lands at `entrypoint/apigen-cli/dist/index.js`, confirmed present and fresh) and spawn it as a child process (`node dist/entrypoint/apigen-cli/index.js run --source ... --type mcp`). Since that path doesn't exist, `node` exits immediately with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, which surfaces to the tests as either an MCP stdio `Connection closed` (-32000) or a "server did not become ready" timeout — **7 failing tests total** (`npx nx test apigen-cli` → 7 failed / 143 passed, verified pre-existing by reverting an unrelated same-session fix in `apigen-core-client` and reproducing identical counts). Not fixed this session (same "outside assigned scope" reasoning as above — the assigned scope for that session was `apigen-core-client`'s Decimal/pnpm regression only). **Fix direction is identical**: these 3 spec files' `BUILT_BIN` constant needs the same one-segment correction (`entrypoint/apigen-cli/dist/index.js`, not `dist/entrypoint/apigen-cli/index.js`) as the executor fix below — likely worth a single shared constant/helper so the path can't drift between the executor and its own test suite again.
**Fix direction:** update `localBin` in `executor.ts:42` to the in-source path (`path.join(context.root, 'entrypoint/apigen-cli/dist/index.js')`), matching every other post-migration in-source dist reference. Low-risk, mechanical, one line — not fixed in this session because the fix lives in `packages/apigen/apigen-generator-nx` (an apigen package), outside this session's explicit dispatch-package-only scope; flagging for a follow-up pass rather than expanding scope unilaterally.
**Status:** OPEN.
**Citations:** [build-plugin-migration worktree, debugger, dispatch-test-regressions + apigen-schema-extraction-pnpm-decimal, 1: packages/apigen/apigen-generator-nx/src/executors/generate/executor.ts:20-45; 2: entrypoint/apigen-cli/project.json:37 (`outputPath: "entrypoint/apigen-cli/dist"`); 3: dist/entrypoint/dispatch-cli/cli/cli.ts:5 (observed broken output, `import * as dispatch-cli_ns from ...`); 4: packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-cli-output/src/lib/generate.ts:139-143 (current, sanitized source); 5: packages/apigen/apigen-engine-naming/src/lib/naming.ts:431-435 (`sanitizeIdentifier`, correct in source); 6: `npx @adhd/apigen-cli --version` → `0.1.0` (live registry fetch, terminal output 2026-07-21); 7: entrypoint/apigen-cli/src/test/e2e/bug-apigen-033-anonymous-default-dispatch.spec.ts:38-43; 8: entrypoint/apigen-cli/src/test/e2e/import-source-cjs-format.spec.ts:3,30-33; 9: entrypoint/apigen-cli/src/test/e2e/real-consumer.spec.ts:52-62; 10: terminal output 2026-07-21, `npx nx test apigen-cli` → 7 failed/143 passed, identical with the Decimal fix present vs. `git apply -R`'d]

### DEBT-DISPATCH-PUBLISH-GATE-001 — no permanent CI gate enforces dispatch package-name/import/dep resolvability

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-15, while closing out BUG-DISPATCH-PUBLISH-001.
**Where:** repo-wide — no script currently runs `npm pack --dry-run` (or equivalent) against the built dispatch packages as part of `test`/CI.
**Impact:** the exact class of bug just fixed (tsconfig aliases masking a real publish break) could silently regress if a future import reintroduces a short/aliased specifier, since in-repo `build`/`test` only exercise the tsconfig path-mapped resolution, never real npm dependency resolution.
**Fix direction:** add a `verify-publish` (or similar) target — per dispatch package (and ideally every publishable `@adhd/*` package), run `npm pack --dry-run --json` against `dist/<projectRoot>` and assert every `dependencies`/`peerDependencies` key is either a real npm registry package or a real `@adhd/*` package name found elsewhere in the workspace (cross-check against the full list of `package.json` `name` fields under `packages/**` and `entrypoint/**`). Wire it into `nx-release-publish`'s `dependsOn` (or a pre-publish check) so a broken publish can never ship again.
**Status:** OPEN.

### DEBT-RELEASE-BASELINE-TAGS-001 — `nx release` changed-only detection has no baseline; first release necessarily touches all projects, and explicit `patch|minor|major` bypasses change-detection

**Status:** PARTIAL

**Discovered:** 2026-07-20, verifying the `nx release` config landed in `58d47a3e` (config itself is structurally correct: `projectsRelationship:independent`, `releaseTagPattern:"{projectName}@{version}"`, `version.generatorOptions:{currentVersionResolver:git-tag, fallbackCurrentVersionResolver:disk, specifierSource:conventional-commits}`, `nx-release-publish` gated on `verify-dist-load`).
**Where:** repo-wide release state — **zero git tags match `{projectName}@{version}`** (`git tag -l | grep -cE '@[0-9]'` → 0)[1].
**Symptom / evidence (both verified via `--dry-run`):**
- (a) With **no baseline tags**, `currentVersionResolver:git-tag` always reports *"Unable to resolve the current version from git tag … Falling back to the version on disk"* for every project, and `specifierSource:conventional-commits` resolves the bump from the **entire** git history (no `@version` boundary to diff against) → the first `nx release` bumps + tags **all ~51** projects, not "only changed since last publish." This is unavoidable bootstrapping, but it means the changed-only guarantee **cannot function until a first baseline release exists**, and that first release is **unproven end-to-end** (no real publish has been run).
- (b) **Semantic mismatch with the stated command:** `nx release patch|minor|major` (an *explicit* specifier — exactly what the request named) bypasses change-detection and bumps **every** project. The changed-only behavior comes from the **bare** `nx release` (no explicit specifier), which lets conventional-commits skip projects with no relevant commits since their tag. Confirmed in dry-run: explicit `patch` → all bump `→0.0.2`; bare (conventional) → per-project `minor` from history. So the user's literal `nx release patch` will NOT restrict to changed items.
**Fix direction:**
1. **Establish baseline tags** without publishing — e.g. `nx release --first-release` semantics, or tag each publishable project at its current disk version (`<project>@<version>`) so subsequent releases diff against it. **Mutating + push-to-remote = requires human approval; not done unilaterally.**
2. **Reconcile the invocation** with intent: document (PUBLISHING.md) that changed-only = bare `nx release`, and explicit `patch|minor|major` = force-bump-all; OR add a thin wrapper/target that maps a requested level onto only the changed projects (`--projects` computed from `nx show projects --affected` against the last release tag).
3. **Prove end-to-end once:** after baselines exist, a `--dry-run` of the bare release must show unchanged projects **skipped** (`🚫 No changes`) — currently unprovable (no tags). Then one real dry-run of `nx release publish`.
**Update 2026-07-20 (bootstrapped + proven locally):**
- **Baseline tags established** — 52 local `{projectName}@{version}` tags at current disk versions (HEAD), matching the exact unscoped project names nx releases (`agent-mcp@2.1.1`, `apigen-cli@0.1.0`, …). **Local only; NOT pushed** (`git push --tags` is human-approval-gated).
- **Changed-only now PROVEN with teeth** (all via `--dry-run`): (i) no-change control — bare `nx release version` → **52/52 resolve version from git tag, 0 bumped, all `🚫 Skipping`**; (ii) positive control — moving `apigen-core-client@0.1.0`'s tag back before commit `6399c4bc` → **exactly 1 project bumps (0.1.0→0.2.0), 51 skipped**, dependents' dep-refs updated via `updateDependents:auto` (tag then restored to HEAD); (iii) negative control — with no tags, all 52 fell back to disk and bumped. So the mechanism reads per-project history-since-tag correctly.
- **Config defect fixed in passing** (`aa24eea1`): the release `projects` exclusions `!@adhd/ui-react-base-storybook` / `!@adhd/environment-cli` used **scoped npm names**, but nx's `projects` filter matches **unscoped project names**, so both `private:true` projects leaked into the release set (54→52 after fix).
- **PUBLISHING.md corrected** (`b5bcfcc5`): documented that `patch|minor|major` force-bumps ALL (changed-only = bare `nx release`), and the tag pattern is unscoped.
**npm-state check (2026-07-20) — settled decision #1.** Queried npm for all 52 release-group packages vs their current disk version: **11 ALREADY published at the disk version** (all `agent-*` @2.1.0/2.1.1, `apigen-cli@0.1.0`, `apigen-codegen-openapi@0.1.1`); **8 published but disk is AHEAD of npm** (`apigen-plugin-{api-express,api-fastify,cli-output,jsonschema,mcp}` disk 0.1.1 > npm 0.1.0; `apigen-plugin-{health,logger,openapi}` disk 0.1.2 > npm 0.1.1); **33 NEVER published (npm 404)** (all `dispatch-*`, `environment*`, `data-*`, `workspace-*`, `ui-react-base-hooks`, `decompile-cli`, 3 `agent-plugin-*`, remaining `apigen-*`). ⇒ blanket "tag at disk version" was correct for only 11/52; for the other 41 a baseline tag at the disk version would **silently suppress their publish**.
**Baseline CORRECTED (2026-07-20):** deleted the 41 wrong tags; **kept only the 11 already-published tags** (at their exact published versions). Re-proven via bare `nx release version --dry-run`: **11 resolve-from-tag → `🚫 Skipping`; 41 (no tag) → selected for release** (fall back to disk, conventional-commits bump). This is the honest bootstrap: "these 11 are on npm; the other 41 are unpublished and ship on the next release." (Surfaced BUILD-CONSIST-011 en route: `data-*`/`ui-react-base-hooks` dist is a version ahead of source — **FIXED 2026-07-20** at `2d51aa47`, see CHANGELOG.md.)
**Remaining before a real publish (genuine human/policy decisions):** (1) **First publish of the 41 — pick the target version policy:** bare `nx release` bumps them via conventional-commits from full history (e.g. `dispatch-serializer-json 0.0.1→0.1.0`), OR `nx release --first-release` ships their *current disk* versions as-is. (2) ~~Fix BUILD-CONSIST-011~~ — **DONE 2026-07-20** (`2d51aa47`; `generatePackageJson: true` on the 3 affected projects, `source==dist` re-verified across all 53 buildable packages). (3) **push tags** (`git push --tags`, human-approved) so CI sees changed-only. (4) one real `nx release publish --dry-run` end-to-end once (1)/(3) settled.
**Status:** PARTIALLY RESOLVED — changed-only detection functional + proven locally; baseline now honest (11 correct tags). Publish gated on the policy decisions above. Does not block the separately-proven "build fixes."
**Citations:** [main@b5bcfcc5, claude, env/release-verification, 1: `git tag -l` + `npx nx release version [patch|--] --dry-run` (no-change + positive-control) 2026-07-20; nx.json:227-273 release config; aa24eea1 exclusion fix; b5bcfcc5 PUBLISHING.md]

### BUG-WORKSPACE-NO-LINKING-001 — repo has NO yarn/npm workspace linking (`node_modules/@adhd/*` doesn't exist); any `@nx/js:tsc`-built package with a runtime (non-type-only) `@adhd/*` dependency throws `Cannot find module`/`ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` from its own dist

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-20, devops-engineer session, while investigating whether `@nx/js:tsc` (unbundled compile) was a viable alternative fix for `INVESTIGATION-BUILD-TOOL-001`'s bundling defect.
- **Root cause:** root `package.json` has **no `workspaces` field** at all (`{"name": "@adhd/source", "private": true}` — no `workspaces: [...]`), and `.yarnrc.yml` sets `nodeLinker: node-modules`. Confirmed directly: `ls node_modules/@adhd/` → does not exist; `node_modules/@adhd/apigen-base-logical`, `node_modules/@adhd/agent-base-types`, etc. have no symlinks. `@adhd/*` imports resolve **only** via Nx's `tsconfig.base.json` path mapping at build time (followed by Vite's `nxViteTsPaths()` plugin, or by `tsc`'s own path resolution during compilation) — there is no runtime linking mechanism at all.
- **Impact:** any package built with `@nx/js:tsc` (which does NOT bundle — it transpiles 1:1, leaving `require('@adhd/x')`/`import ... from '@adhd/x'` as literal specifiers in the compiled output) that has a REAL (non-type-only) runtime dependency on another `@adhd/*` package will throw when its dist is actually loaded, because Node has nothing to resolve `@adhd/x` against. Type-only `@adhd/*` imports are silently safe (erased at compile time, e.g. `agent-core-provider`'s `@adhd/agent-base-types` import is comment-only in its compiled output) — this is why some existing `@nx/js:tsc` packages already pass `verify-dist-load` and others don't; it's invisible until a package's dependency usage crosses from types-only to runtime.
- **Confirmed instances (via `npx nx run-many -t verify-dist-load --all`, 2026-07-20):**
  - `agent-mcp` — `Error [ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND]: Cannot find package '@adhd/agent-store-runtime'` imported from `dist/entrypoint/agent-mcp/src/index.js`.
  - `decompile-cli` — `Cannot find package '@adhd/data-base-transforms'` imported from `dist/entrypoint/decompile-cli/src/lib/extractors/index.js`.
  - `agent-engine-compiler` — `Cannot find package '@adhd/agent-store-prompts'` imported from `dist/packages/agent/agent-engine-compiler/src/resolve/composition.js`.
  - `agent-engine-orchestrator` — `Cannot find package '@adhd/agent-store-runtime'` imported from `dist/packages/agent/agent-engine-orchestrator/src/engine/dag-engine.js`.
  - Reproduced directly while prototyping: converting `apigen-core-client` (which has a real runtime `@adhd/apigen-base-logical` dependency) from `@nx/vite:build` to `@nx/js:tsc` immediately hit `Error: Cannot find module '@adhd/apigen-base-logical'` from its own compiled `compose-schemas.js` — confirming this is a structural property of the repo, not specific to the 4 packages above (converting `apigen-core-client` to tsc was reverted in favor of the `external`-config fix in `INVESTIGATION-BUILD-TOOL-001`, precisely because of this finding).
- **Why this is NOT fixed by `INVESTIGATION-BUILD-TOOL-001`'s resolution:** that item's fix (`tools/vite-external-deps.mjs`) only applies to `@nx/vite:build` packages — it works specifically BECAUSE it keeps bundling `@adhd/*` source (the only thing that currently makes cross-package runtime imports work) while externalizing real npm deps. The 4 packages above are already on `@nx/js:tsc` (chosen for other good reasons — native `better-sqlite3` addons / Nx devkit plugin-loading constraints, see `BUILD-CONSIST-008`) and have no bundling step to lean on; they need REAL workspace linking, which this repo does not have.
- **Fix direction (not attempted this session — separate, larger initiative):** either (a) add a `workspaces` field to root `package.json` and switch `.yarnrc.yml`'s `nodeLinker` appropriately so `yarn install` symlinks every `packages/**`/`entrypoint/**` package with its own `package.json` into `node_modules/@adhd/*` (the standard monorepo mechanism — but broad, repo-wide, and this repo's `packages/**` dirs are NOT the npm package roots on disk, `dist/{projectRoot}` is, per `nx-release-publish`'s `packageRoot` option, so workspace-linking source dirs may not even point at what actually gets consumed downstream — needs careful design); or (b) a build/postinstall step that symlinks each `@adhd/*` package's `dist/` output into `node_modules/@adhd/*` so `@nx/js:tsc`-built packages can resolve siblings the way a real npm consumer would (closer to how `nx-release-publish`'s `packageRoot: "dist/{projectRoot}"` already treats dist as the real package root); or (c) audit whether the 4 tsc packages above should switch to `@nx/vite:build` + `externalizeRealDeps` instead (accepting the native-addon-bundling risk `BUILD-CONSIST-008` warned about, and re-verifying it's actually safe for these 4 specifically).
- **Cross-references:** `INVESTIGATION-BUILD-TOOL-001` (the related-but-distinct bundling defect, resolved this session), `BUILD-CONSIST-008` (why these 4 are on tsc in the first place), `BUG-YARN-LOCKFILE-MISMATCH-001` / the pnpm-migration decision (a pnpm migration would very likely introduce real workspace linking as a side effect — worth coordinating these two initiatives rather than solving linking twice).
- **Status:** OPEN — root cause confirmed with 4 concrete failing instances; fix deferred (structural monorepo-linking decision, out of scope for a bundling-config fix).

### BUG-BUILD-TSC-STALE-DIST-METADATA-001 — 3 `@nx/js:tsc` packages declare a `package.json` `"module"` (ESM) entry their build never produces

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-20, `npx nx run-many -t verify-dist-load --all` full-workspace audit (devops-engineer session).
- **Where/evidence:** `workspace-base-tools`, `workspace-codegen-nx`, `apigen-generator-nx` — each: CJS/`main` entry (`dist/.../src/index.js`) loads cleanly, but the declared `module` field (`dist/.../index.mjs`) fails `verify-dist-load` with `Error: file declared in package.json but missing from dist — build did not produce it`. The `@nx/js:tsc` executor these 3 use emits only the single format configured in `tsconfig.lib.json` (CJS here) — nothing ever writes `index.mjs` — so the `package.json`'s `"module": "./index.mjs"` field (or equivalent) is stale/aspirational metadata.
- **Impact:** low-severity today (the real, resolvable `main` entry works), but it means `verify-dist-load` currently reports these 3 as failing even though their actual consumer-facing entry is fine — noise that could mask a REAL failure if these get fixed by just deleting the stale field without checking. Also: any ESM-only consumer (`import` with no `require` fallback) that reads `package.json`'s `module`/`exports.import` field would 404.
- **Fix direction:** remove the dangling `module`/ESM `exports` field from each of the 3 `package.json`s (they're CJS-only builds; declaring an entry the build can't produce is simply wrong), OR configure `@nx/js:tsc` to actually emit dual CJS+ESM if these packages need both. Low-risk, mechanical — did not fix this session because it's outside the two assigned findings' scope.
- **Status:** OPEN.

### BUG-DATA-CORE-STRUCTURES-DIST-TYPE-MISMATCH-001 — `data-core-structures`' CJS `main` entry is CommonJS source under a `package.json` that declares `"type": "module"`

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-20, `npx nx run-many -t verify-dist-load --all` full-workspace audit.
- **Evidence:** `npx nx run data-core-structures:verify-dist-load` — `main` (`dist/packages/data/data-core-structures/index.js`) throws `ReferenceError: exports is not defined in ES module scope` ("This file is being treated as an ES module because it has a '.js' file extension and '.../package.json' contains \"type\": \"module\". To treat it as a CommonJS script, rename it to use the '.cjs' file extension." — Node's own diagnostic). The `module` (`index.mjs`) entry loads cleanly.
- **Impact:** any consumer that `require()`s `@adhd/data-core-structures` (the `main` field, the default resolution for a bare `require()` or a bundler targeting CJS) gets a hard crash. Only ESM `import` consumers work today.
- **Fix direction:** either emit `index.js` as actual ESM syntax to match the declared `"type": "module"`, or emit the CJS build as `index.cjs` (and repoint `main`) so Node's own extension-based module-type detection matches the file's real syntax. Needs the same audit as `INVESTIGATION-BUILD-TOOL-001` gave the apigen packages, applied to `data-core-structures`'s own build config — not fixed this session (outside the two assigned findings' scope).
- **Status:** OPEN.

### BUG-APIGEN-CLI-VERIFY-DIST-LOAD-ARGV-001 — `apigen-cli`'s dist entry runs CLI argv-parsing as a side effect of module load, so `verify-dist-load`'s `require()` always fails

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-20, `npx nx run apigen-cli:verify-dist-load` (after fixing an unrelated `sync-deps` version-drift lint failure that was independently blocking this project's build).
- **Evidence:** `node ./scripts/verify-dist-load.mjs entrypoint/apigen-cli` → `error: unknown command 'entrypoint/apigen-cli'` — the CLI's bin entry parses `process.argv` at module-load time (no `if (require.main === module)` / `import.meta.main` guard), so simply `require()`/`import()`-ing the built module (exactly what `verify-dist-load` does, and exactly what a real consumer's `require()` would also trigger) makes it try to interpret the test harness's own argv as a subcommand and fail.
- **Impact:** `apigen-cli` cannot pass `verify-dist-load` as currently written regardless of how correct its bundle is — this is a false negative in the specific sense that it's not a bundling defect, but it's a REAL defect in the sense that `require()`ing this package for any purpose other than "run it as the top-level process" has a crashing side effect, which is not how a well-behaved library/CLI-with-a-programmatic-API entry should behave.
- **Fix direction:** guard the CLI's top-level `main()`/argv-parsing invocation so it only runs when the module is the actual process entry point (not when `require()`/`import()`-ed as a dependency) — standard Node CLI hygiene (`if (require.main === module)` for CJS, `import.meta.main` — Node 22.14+ — or a `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) === process.argv[1]` check for ESM). Not fixed this session (outside the two assigned findings' scope; found while unblocking `apigen-cli`'s lint for verification purposes).
- **Status:** OPEN.

### BUG-CI-PUBLISH-STALE-TARGETS-001 — CI's "Publish" job does not use `nx release` at all; it calls vestigial `version`/`publish` targets that hardcode a nonexistent `dist/libs/core` path

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-20, devops-engineer session, cross-checking `PUBLISHING.md`'s "CI publish (automated)" section against the actual `.github/workflows/pull-request.yml`.
- **Evidence:** `PUBLISHING.md` documents CI as running `npx nx release version --dry-run` / `npx nx release publish --dry-run`. The actual workflow (`.github/workflows/pull-request.yml`, `Publish` step, gated on a `publish` PR label or merge) runs `npx nx affected -t build/version/publish --exclude=$EXCLUDE --configuration=production` — the **legacy** `version`/`publish` `targetDefaults` in `nx.json` (`nx.json:143-190`), not `nx release`. Those legacy targets' `production` configuration hardcodes `npm publish dist/libs/core` — a path with no corresponding project anywhere in this workspace (`libs/` doesn't exist; packages live under `packages/`/`entrypoint/`). This predates the `nx release` migration (`58d47a3e`) and was never removed/rewired.
- **Impact:** if the CI "Publish" job ever actually ran (a PR gets the `publish` label, or gets merged) with affected libraries present, `npm publish dist/libs/core` would fail outright (no such directory) — the job is effectively dead/non-functional, and does not benefit from ANY of this session's `verify-dist-load` gating work (`BUG-RELEASE-PUBLISH-GATE-BYPASS-001`'s fix, `scripts/release-publish.mjs`, is not wired into CI at all). `PUBLISHING.md`'s CI section is currently aspirational, not accurate.
- **Fix direction:** rewire the CI `Publish` step to call `node scripts/release-publish.mjs` (this session's Finding-1 fix) instead of the legacy `nx affected -t version/publish` targets, and remove or clearly deprecate the vestigial `version`/`publish` `targetDefaults` in `nx.json` so they can't be reached by accident again. **Not done this session** — rewiring a live npm-publishing GitHub Actions job (uses the `NPM_TOKEN` secret, runs on real PR merges) is a distinct, higher-risk change that needs explicit human sign-off, separate from the `nx release`-path fix this session delivered.
- **Status:** OPEN.

### BUG-ENVPLAN-020 — `adhd-environment` `builder-snapshot-api` guard requires the raw `src` package path, which nx never populates → guard can never pass

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-16, executing `adhd-environment` wave 3 (`builder-snapshot-api`) via plan-orchestrator → typescript-pro.
**Where:** `docs/plan/adhd-environment/dag.json`, `builder-snapshot-api.guard` — final step is `node -e 'const{build}=require("./packages/environment/environment-builder"); …'`.
**Symptom:** the implementation is fully correct (139/139 tests pass; the *identical* check against `./dist/packages/environment/environment-builder` returns exit 0, methods ok), but the guard's raw-`src` require throws `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, so the guard exits 1 and the state cannot be completed. Verified state-side: state left `in_progress` (executor correctly refused to mark complete on a red guard).
**Root cause:** every `@adhd/*` package (40+) uses `"main":"./index.js"`, valid only once copied to `dist/packages/<path>/<name>/` (`nx build` populates dist, never the src package root; cf. `PUBLISHING.md`). Editing the src `package.json` `main` is a false fix — `dist/package.json` is a byte copy, so it would break the published package's own resolution.
**Fix direction:** change the guard's require target from `./packages/environment/environment-builder` → `./dist/packages/environment/environment-builder` (matches how every package in the repo is actually consumed). **Audit sibling states** for the same class: any `adhd-environment` guard that `require()`s a raw `src` package path (per ENV-PLAN-011, all three audit gates are `nx build`-based). Plan/guard defect — must be repaired by plan-builder (guards are not hand-editable per orchestrator rules); the executor's implementation is done and dist-verified.
**Status:** OPEN — blocks `builder-snapshot-api` completion and the downstream adhd-environment waves.

### DEBT-ENVBUILD-001 — `environment-builder/vite.config.ts` externalized no node builtins (latent build break); audit sibling `platform:node` packages

**Status:** MIXED

**Discovered:** 2026-07-16, same execution — surfaced once the real snapshot pipeline (which imports `node:fs`/`path`/`os`/`crypto`) was wired through `index.ts`.
**Where:** `packages/environment/environment-builder/vite.config.ts` — `rollupOptions.external` was `[]`, so Vite tried to browser-externalize `node:*`, breaking `nx build` with `"readFileSync" is not exported by "__vite-browser-external"`.
**Status:** FIXED in working tree by the executor (outside its 3 reserved files, disclosed) using the established `external: [/^node:/]` pattern from `packages/apigen/python-env/vite.config.ts`. **Follow-up (OPEN):** audit the other newly-scaffolded `platform:node` packages in this plan family (`environment-core-node`, `environment-cli`) for the same empty-`external` defect before their builder states run.

### BUG-AGENTMCP-001 — the built `dist` agent-mcp server cannot resolve its own `@adhd/*` workspace deps at runtime (`.mcp.json`'s dev entry is dead)

**Status:** MIXED

**Discovered:** 2026-07-11, while registering agent-mcp as a global (user-scope) Claude Code MCP server.
**Symptom:** running the dev entry from `.mcp.json` (`node dist/entrypoint/agent-mcp/src/index.js`, the `agent-mcp` server) exits immediately:
```
Error [ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND]: Cannot find package '@adhd/agent-store-runtime'
  imported from <repo>/dist/entrypoint/agent-mcp/src/index.js
```
Reproduced with a real MCP stdio handshake (initialize + tools/list) — the process dies before emitting any JSON-RPC, so the host sees a server that never connects.
**Root cause:** `@adhd/*` specifiers are resolved **only by tsconfig `paths` at build time**. At runtime Node does ordinary `node_modules` resolution and finds nothing: root `package.json` declares **no `workspaces` field** (packageManager is `yarn@4.5.3`), so `node_modules/@adhd/` exists but is **empty** — no workspace links were ever created. `@nx/js:tsc` emits the bare `@adhd/…` imports verbatim into `dist/` and generates no package.json with resolvable deps, so every built entrypoint that imports a workspace package is affected — not just agent-mcp.
**Consequences:** the `agent-mcp` entry in `.mcp.json` has presumably never worked; only `agent-mcp-published` (`npx -y @adhd/agent-mcp@latest`, deps resolved from the npm registry) does. Any check that imports the source or runs through vite/vitest (both honor tsconfig paths) passes while the shipped artifact is broken — exactly the bypass CLAUDE.md §6 warns about.
**Fix direction (in order of preference):**
1. Add a `workspaces` field to root `package.json` so the package manager links `@adhd/*` into `node_modules/` (most standard; fixes every entrypoint at once).
2. Enable `generatePackageJson` on the `@nx/js:tsc` build targets so `dist/entrypoint/agent-mcp/package.json` carries resolvable deps.
3. A `dist/node_modules/@adhd/<name> -> ../../packages/<group>/<name>` link step in the build (contained, but a workaround).

**Do NOT** "fix" this by having a test import the built server's functions directly — that reaches inside the server instead of calling it like a host, and would keep the real defect green.
**Re-confirmed 2026-07-22 (post-pnpm-migration), while verifying BUG-AGENTMCP-REGISTRY-DB-CANTOPEN-001's fix against a real built host:** the exact repro still fires verbatim today — `node dist/entrypoint/agent-mcp/src/index.js` (the literal path in `.mcp.json`'s `agent-mcp` entry, run from repo root) still throws `Error [ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND]: Cannot find package '@adhd/agent-store-runtime'`[1]. BUT the underlying root cause is now **stale**, not live: fix direction 1 (a package-manager-linked `node_modules/@adhd/*`) already landed via the yarn→pnpm migration (project memory `project_pnpm_insource_dist_migration`) — pnpm workspace links now exist and the **in-source** dist target (`entrypoint/agent-mcp/dist/src/index.js`, `@nx/js:tsc`'s real `outputPath` per `entrypoint/agent-mcp/project.json`) resolves and runs perfectly against the exact same env[2]. The real, current defect is narrower than originally scoped: `.mcp.json`'s `agent-mcp` (dev, non-published) entry's `args` still points at the **pre-migration root-level** `dist/entrypoint/agent-mcp/src/index.js` — a stale, orphaned build output (confirmed gitignored + untracked, mtime frozen at the last time something built to the old root `dist/` convention, predating today's session) that never receives new builds and does not even contain BUG-AGENTMCP-REGISTRY-DB-CANTOPEN-001's fix (still has the literal pre-fix `fileMustExist: true` line)[3]. This is the same failure class as `BUG-AGENTMCP-002` (stale dist trees) but specifically explains WHY `.mcp.json`'s dev entry is dead: not missing workspace links (fixed), but a config path frozen at a retired output location.
**Updated fix direction:** repoint `.mcp.json`'s `agent-mcp` entry `args` from `dist/entrypoint/agent-mcp/src/index.js` to `entrypoint/agent-mcp/dist/src/index.js` (the real, current, in-source-dist build output already produced by plain `npx nx build agent-mcp`) — no workspace/build-system change needed, just the path. The original fix directions 1-3 above are effectively superseded by the pnpm migration and can be struck once this is verified end-to-end.
**Status:** OPEN (narrowed scope; underlying module-resolution root cause resolved by the pnpm migration, remaining defect is `.mcp.json`'s stale path).

### BUG-AGENTMCP-002 — stale `dist/` trees from a pre-move layout shadow current packages with duplicate `@adhd/*` names

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-07-11, while mapping `@adhd/*` name → `dist/` output path to work around `BUG-AGENTMCP-001`.
**Symptom:** `dist/packages/ai/*`, `dist/packages/shared/*`, and `dist/packages/node-tools/*` are leftover build outputs from an older directory layout that no longer exists in source. They carry **duplicate package names** against the live `dist/packages/agent/*` outputs — e.g. `@adhd/agent-mcp` resolves to both `dist/entrypoint/agent-mcp` and `dist/packages/ai/agent-mcp`; `@adhd/agent-mcp-types` to both `dist/packages/ai/…` and `dist/packages/shared/…`; `@adhd/agent-mcp-budget` to both `dist/packages/ai/…` and `dist/packages/node-tools/…`.
**Impact:** benign today (nothing resolves by scanning `dist/`), but it makes any name-keyed dist resolution — including fix option (3) of `BUG-AGENTMCP-001` — ambiguous and non-deterministic, and makes `dist/` misleading to read. Any link map must be derived from the **source** tree (`packages/*/*/package.json` → `dist/<projectRoot>`), never by globbing `dist/`.
**Fix direction:** `npx nx reset` + a clean full build, then confirm the stale group dirs do not reappear (they should not — no source project maps to them). Deliberately **not** deleted at discovery time: removing directories is destructive and requires human approval per CLAUDE.md.
**Status:** OPEN.

### BUG-APIGEN-002 — generated MCP servers / CLIs can't resolve `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` when run outside the repo tree

**Status:** MIXED

**Discovered:** 2026-06-23, while verifying BUG-APIGEN-001 via the dod.2 (`generate-parity`) and dod.cli (`cli-output`) probes.
**Symptom:** `probe_mcp.mjs generate-parity` / `cli-output` write the generated `server.ts` / `cli.ts` to an OS tmpdir, then run it with `npx tsx <file>` (cwd=REPO_ROOT). The generated file's bare import `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/index.js` throws `Error: Cannot find module … code: 'MODULE_NOT_FOUND'`, so the MCP client sees `McpError -32000: Connection closed` and the probe exits non-zero.
**Root cause:** Node/tsx resolve bare specifiers from the *generated file's* directory upward. The tmpdir has no `node_modules` ancestry to the repo, and `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` is a real npm package (not a `@adhd/*` tsconfig path alias), so it never resolves. The `generate` command (`packages/apigen/cli/src/lib/commands/generate.ts`) emits only the plugin's `.ts` files — no `node_modules` symlink, no `tsconfig.json`, no `package.json` — so generated output is not self-resolving anywhere except inside the repo tree.
**Proof it is independent of BUG-APIGEN-001:** restoring the pristine (pre-fix) `core`+`runtime` files and rebuilding reproduces the *identical* `Cannot find module '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/index.js'` failure. The error is thrown at the generated file's import phase, before any dispatch/ctx code runs.
**Fix direction (NOT done — outside this task's core+runtime scope):** make `generate` emit resolution scaffolding into `--out-dir` — e.g. symlink the repo `node_modules` into the out-dir and emit a `tsconfig.json` that maps `@adhd/*` to the repo source — so generated servers/CLIs run anywhere. Lives in `packages/apigen/cli` (+ possibly the mcp/cli plugin templates), not in core/runtime.
**Status:** RESOLVED/VERIFIED 2026-06-23 (reconciled by pseudosky) — fixed in apigen-v2 via the **Option-A "publish" model**: `generate` emits a clean publishable `package.json` with real `^<version>` deps (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`, `@adhd/apigen-runtime`/-core) + `tsconfig.json`; the pre-publish workspace bridge is the default-off `--link-workspace` flag. dod.2 (`generate-parity`) and dod.cli (`cli-output`) pass inside the apigen-client-generation **final audit 117/117** (re-verified green this session). **Follow-on (open, tracked):** the per-surface 3rd-party **dep-manifest emission** for logical/rich types is owned by `docs/plan/apigen-logical-types` → state `lt-dep-manifest` / `[dod.gen-deps]`. (Earlier OPEN status was stale relative to the completed plan.)

### DEBT-APIGEN-007 — `lt-dep-manifest` dep-collection is end-to-end blocked by missing `lt-extract-scalars`

**Status:** OPEN

**Discovered:** 2026-06-25, during `lt-dep-manifest` state execution.
**Symptom:** The dep-manifest machinery (`collectFormats` → `collectLogicalTypeDeps` → `patchPackageJsonDeps`) is implemented and works for schemas that carry `format: decimal`. However, when a TS source file uses `DecimalValue = string` (or any type alias that resolves to a primitive), `ts-morph`'s `p.getType().getText()` resolves the alias to `string` BEFORE passing the type text to `buildSchema` / `ts-json-schema-generator`. The `format` annotation on the alias is lost. The dep-collection step sees `{type: 'string'}` instead of `{type: 'string', format: 'decimal'}` and emits no dep.
**Root cause:** `packages/apigen/core/src/lib/extractors/named.ts` calls `p.getType().getText()` which eagerly resolves type aliases. Changing it to `p.getTypeNode()?.getText()` (the alias name) would preserve alias identity, allowing `ts-json-schema-generator` to look up the `@format` annotation.
**Fix direction:** in `lt-extract-scalars` (state in the `apigen-logical-types` plan): change `packages/apigen/apigen-core-client/src/lib/extractors/named.ts` to preserve the alias name (use `getTypeNode()?.getText()` for the type text), and add `DecimalString`/`DecimalValue` to `SCALAR_SCHEMAS` in `packages/apigen/apigen-core-client/src/lib/schema-builders/ts-json-schema.ts` (or rely on `ts-json-schema-generator` picking up the `@format` JSDoc from the exported type alias). Once extraction preserves format annotations, the `lt-dep-manifest` machinery activates end-to-end without further changes.
**Status:** OPEN — `lt-dep-manifest` infrastructure is green; activation gate is `lt-extract-scalars`. **tracked-by: `docs/plan/apigen-logical-types` → state `lt-extract-scalars`.**

### DEFER-PYGRPC-001 — gRPC-Web support (sonora/grpclib) not included in py-grpc target

**Status:** DEFERRED

**Discovered:** 2026-06-25, while implementing the py-grpc apigen target.
**Detail:** gRPC-Web requires an HTTP/1.1-to-gRPC proxy or a pure-Python gRPC-Web server (e.g. `sonora`). `grpcio` alone serves native gRPC (HTTP/2); browser clients need gRPC-Web. The pure-Python option `sonora` exists (pip installable) but adds a non-trivial ASGI/WSGI dependency and was not verified in this env.
**When to address:** when browser-side gRPC-Web consumers are required. At that point: (a) verify `pip install sonora`; (b) add a `grpc_web` optional group to pyproject.toml; (c) wrap the server in a `sonora.asgi.grpcASGI` + uvicorn layer alongside the grpcio server, or use an Envoy sidecar.
**Status:** DEFERRED.

### FEAT-APIGEN-001 — Rust / Go / Java host languages — priority: HIGH

**Status:** UNKNOWN
**Priority:** HIGH

- **What:** apigen treats Rust/Go/Java as first-class at the *contract* level — declared in `PluginLanguage` (`packages/apigen/core/src/lib/types.ts`), routed by `source-language.ts` (`.rs`/`.go`/`.java`), wire mapped per-type in the DESIGN (§13.2), with a host-runbook generator (`packages/apigen/nx/src/generators/host`). But there is **zero implementation**: no extractor, runtime, server plugin, or filled codec columns. Only TS + Python hosts exist (proven byte-equal).
- **Deliverable per language:** fill that language's template-cell column (encode/decode/imports/dep/mode per logical type) + an extractor (source → operations) + a runtime (validate→dispatch→wire via the language's logical codecs) + an HTTP server target (analogous to `py-flask`) + go green on the cross-host conformance gate (`apigen-conformance:conformance`).
- **Order:** Java first (cheapest — DESIGN notes one dep, `jackson-datatype-jsr310`, rest stdlib Jackson), then Go, then Rust.
- **Acceptance:** `apigen serve --source a.ts --source b.py --source c.java` round-trips a Date/Decimal **byte-identically** across all three; conformance matrix green for each new host.

### FEAT-WORKSPACE-001 — workspace-standards base generator + custom workspace lint — priority: HIGH (foundational)

**Status:** UNKNOWN
**Priority:** HIGH

**Goal:** every nx project/target in the monorepo conforms to one enforced standard, generated by inheritance and verified by a custom workspace lint.

**Every project must:**
- Define the standard nx **targets**: `build`, `lint`, `test` — plus `demo`/`verify` (drives the live use-driven gate; ties to the live-testing rule in CLAUDE.md) and `nx-release-publish` (publishable libs) with `dependsOn:[build,test]`.
- Contain the standard **files**: `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (per-project agent invariants/footguns), `DEMO.md` (runnable; the `demo` target drives it), `CHANGELOG.md` (Keep-a-Changelog + conventional commits), and a `PLAYBOOK.md` (pre- and post-merge requirements).
- Carry standard `project.json` **conventions**: `tags` for `layer:*` + `platform:*` (architecture enforcement), correct `dependsOn` ordering, `publishConfig.access:public` for publishable libs.

**Base generator (enforcement by inheritance):** a `@adhd/workspace-base` generator package that ALL other nx generators compose/inherit, so the targets+files+conventions are generated automatically and can't be forgotten. **The base must support an `upgrade`/migration** that back-fills and updates every existing project when the base changes (`nx g @adhd/workspace-base:upgrade` → idempotent sync across all projects).

**Custom workspace lint (the gate):** a repo-level check (custom nx target / lint rule / CI gate) that FAILS if any project is missing a required target, a required file, or a required `project.json` field — so the standard is *enforced*, not merely generated.

**Additional standards (proposed):**
- A `verify` aggregate target = `lint` + `test` + `demo`, required to pass pre-merge.
- DEMO.md must be runnable end-to-end; the `demo` target is its CI entry (no env-gating of the demo — per the live-testing rule).
- README declares layer/platform + public API; CLAUDE.md declares the project's invariants/footguns for agents; PLAYBOOK declares pre/post-merge steps (build/lint/test/demo + changelog bump + version stamp).
- CHANGELOG enforced: a changeset/entry required for any `src` change (CI gate).
- tsconfig consistency (extends workspace base; `@adhd/*` path aliases), `.eslintrc` consistency, hyphenated package names, `I`-prefixed shared interfaces (per CLAUDE.md §7).
- CLI projects: `bin` + `#!/usr/bin/env node` shebang convention.
- Test policy: ≥1 **default-running** live/integration test per public feature (per the live-testing rule — gated tests don't satisfy this).

**Update 2026-06-26 (reframed: agent-optimized, core/adapter split).** Full scope:
`docs/workspace-base/SCOPE.md` (rewritten). **Reframe:** optimize the monorepo for *agents, not
human browsing* — a 5-layer decision-routing system (generated index → auto-loaded CLAUDE.md → impact graph →
soft memory → intent router); invariant = "generate the routing table, author the rulebook, keep memory soft,
promote invariants to machine-checkable gates" (memory `01KW2GHJE…`).

**Split into a platform-agnostic core + thin adapters** so the reusable parts aren't confined to nx/Node:
- `@adhd/workspace-standard` — **generalizable core** (pure TS, zero nx import; validates a Python/Rust/Go
  package equally): required-targets registry, required-files+section engine, managed-region engine,
  post-change rule engine, provenance validator, routing-index format + drift gate, boundary-policy *as data*,
  per-package metadata schema, configurable layout map.
- `@adhd/workspace-nx` — **nx adapter**: generators + root post-generate hook, `@adhd/eslint-plugin-workspace`
  (boundary enforcement), `nx affected` impact-graph binding, esbuild/tsconfig stamping, registry drift gate.

Resolved (Q1–Q8): inheritance = automatic-via-root-nx (fallback explicit `applyWorkspaceStandard`); upgrade =
managed-region markers + marker-tag confirmation; gate = eslint workspace rule; scope = all projects +
`exempt`; targets += `typecheck` (non-TS → mypy/pyright); file-content = existence-error + placeholder
warn(dev)/error(prod).

**Config homes (NOT package.json — decoupled from Node):** root taxonomy + layout + boundary policy →
**`.adhd/workspace.json`** (repo-defined; each repo defines its OWN taxonomy — adhd is project-focused, we take
sox's *mechanism* not its db/ml vocabulary; areas/groups carry `description`/`whenToUse`/`examples` so agents
place code from meaning, not guessing). Per-package metadata → **`<pkg>/.adhd/meta.json`** (ecosystem-neutral).
`generate-lib.sh` superseded. **The 5 reframe layers are implemented as generator + linter logic, not docs**
(SCOPE §0a): generator stamps meta + scaffolds CLAUDE.md hierarchy + ROUTER + regenerates routing index;
linter gates index drift, undeclared area/group, missing CLAUDE.md/markers, dangling router targets, boundary
policy, post-change rules, provenance.

**Harvested from sox `memory-refactor` NX-GENERATOR-HANDOFF (their needs align — same generator, more mature
layout):** name decoupled from folder path (integrity/registry key — hard rule); per-package metadata block
`{area,group,concerns,invariants,entrypoints}` as the routing source of truth; two-level `area/group`
taxonomy + depConstraint allow-matrix (`data↛platform`, `shared` is leaf); the `authoring`(core)↔`sox-nx`
(adapter) split (= our core/adapter cut); the 4-point acceptance suite. *adhd contributes back:* required-docs
set + post-change enforcement + provenance + managed markers (sox only stamps a CLAUDE.md stub). The generic
core is intended to be consumed by **both** repos (sox's `sox-nx` becomes a second adapter).

**Broken out as their own features (reusable beyond workspace scaffolding):** see `FEAT-CHANGE-ENFORCE-001`
(post-change enforcement layer) and `FEAT-PROVENANCE-001` (change provenance schema + validator) below.

### FEAT-CHANGE-ENFORCE-001 — post-change enforcement layer — priority: HIGH

Declarative "what must update when you change X" rule engine, git-diff-driven, **nx-free** (lives in

### FEAT-PROVENANCE-001 — change provenance schema + validator — priority: HIGH

**Status:** UNKNOWN
**Priority:** HIGH

Every change carries: work-item id (`plan:`|`backlog:`|`oneoff`) · dispatcher · author `name:version` ·
provider/model. **Carrier = BOTH:** git **commit trailers** (`Work-Item:`/`Author:`/`Model:`) are the
enforceable source written at commit time; the **CHANGELOG** trailer is the human-visible projection generated
from them. **Author identity resolved from the running agent's SP context** (same as `/reflection`:
