### DEBT-BACKLOG-MARKDOWN-DUP-001 — `@adhd/backlog`'s markdown parser will duplicate `tools/util/backlog.mjs`'s parsing algorithm until the latter is deprecated

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-22, same design pass, designing `entrypoint/backlog/src/markdown.ts`'s `importFromMarkdown`/`renderToMarkdown` contract.
- **Detail:** `tools/util/backlog.mjs` (`HEADER_RE`, `classifyStatus`, `detectStatus`, `detectPriority`, `parse` — `tools/util/backlog.mjs:34-155`) is a standalone `tools/` script, not a built/importable package, so `@adhd/backlog`'s `markdown.ts` must **port** (reimplement), not `import`, the same parsing algorithm — a real, acknowledged violation of the "Two-Use Refactor Rule" (`AGENTS.md` §8) that is deliberate for v0.1 (extracting a shared package for a two-line parser duplicated exactly once is premature; DESIGN.md §8 states the reasoning).
- **Impact:** once `@adhd/backlog` ships and is proven equivalent (its own DoD requires round-tripping against the real `tools/util/backlog.mjs` — see `entrypoint/backlog/SPEC.md` §7 item 2), the two implementations will drift if either is edited without the other — `tools/util/backlog.mjs` should be deprecated/removed at that point, not kept indefinitely as a duplicate.
- **Fix direction:** once `@adhd/backlog importFromMarkdown`/`renderToMarkdown` are implemented and proven byte-compatible with `tools/util/backlog.mjs`'s parser (per the DoD), deprecate and remove `tools/util/backlog.mjs`, updating any docs/scripts that still invoke it directly.
- **Update 2026-07-22 (implementation session):** the DoD proof now exists and is green — `entrypoint/backlog/src/markdown.spec.ts` imports the repo's real `BACKLOG.md`, renders it back out, and re-parses the render with `tools/util/backlog.mjs` (real subprocess) confirming every id/status/priority round-trips. Deprecation/removal of `tools/util/backlog.mjs` itself has NOT been done — remains a follow-up action.
- **Update 2026-07-24 (re-scoped, NOT resolved):** `tools/util/backlog.mjs` must stay alive and unremoved through `docs/plan/backlog-adoption/MIGRATION.md`'s Phase 1/2 — it is the live, independent parity oracle both `parity-check.mjs` (Phase 2, now wired into CI non-blocking) and `import-manifest.mjs` (Phase 1) invoke as a subprocess on every run. This item's own fix direction ("deprecate and remove `tools/util/backlog.mjs`") is correct but was under-sequenced — it is now explicitly tracked as `MIGRATION.md` **Phase 5** ("Deprecate the legacy parser"), which only begins once Phases 3–4 (write-path cut-over + skill distribution) are stable. Do not delete the script before Phase 5, and when Phase 5 executes, `markdown.spec.ts`'s round-trip test (which subprocess-invokes this exact script as its oracle) must be rewritten to a different oracle FIRST.
- **Status:** OPEN — DoD proof satisfied; deprecation sequenced under `MIGRATION.md` Phase 5, not yet reached.
- Citations: [main, architect-reviewer, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-design/backlog-adoption, 1: tools/util/backlog.mjs:1-22,34-155; 2: entrypoint/backlog/DESIGN.md §8; 3: entrypoint/backlog/SPEC.md §7 item 2, §4.3; 4: entrypoint/backlog/src/markdown.spec.ts; 5: docs/plan/backlog-adoption/MIGRATION.md Phase 5 (lines 789-802)]

### BUG-APIGEN-ENGINE-RUNTIME-DISPATCH-REF-UNSUPPORTED-001 — `@adhd/apigen-engine-runtime`'s run-mode `dispatch()` cannot handle ANY JSON-Schema `$ref`, regardless of where `definitions` live

**Status:** MIXED

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-22, implementing `entrypoint/backlog/src/server.ts` (apigen MOUNT wiring — extract → composeSchemas → `plugin.run()`). `client.ts`'s return types (`CreateItemResult`, `BacklogItem[]`, ...) reference named exported types (`BacklogStatus`, `Priority`, `Citation`, `Note`) heavily enough that `@adhd/apigen-core-client`'s extraction resolves them via ts-json-schema-generator's named-type ($ref + `definitions`) path rather than fully inlining every occurrence. Calling a real mounted tool (`createItem`, both via MCP `callTool` and via HTTP) crashed with `[apigen-logical] $ref "#/definitions/BacklogStatus" cannot be resolved in run-mode without a descriptor root. Supply a resolve() in the ctx override to handle $ref.`
- **Root cause:** `packages/apigen/apigen-base-logical/src/lib/runmode.ts`'s `buildCtx()` hard-codes `resolve: (ref) => { throw new Error(...) }` — a permanent stub, never a real dereferencer — and `packages/apigen/apigen-engine-runtime/src/lib/dispatch.ts`'s `encodeResult`/`decodeArg` call `_transcoder.encode/decode` via a MODULE-LEVEL frozen transcoder with NO way for a caller (a plugin, a host like `apigen-plugin-mcp`/`apigen-plugin-api-fastify`) to supply a working `resolve()` override. Verified directly: even after hoisting a schema's nested `definitions` up to its own top level (where AJV, a *different* consumer, would resolve it fine), `dispatch()`'s transcoder still throws — the resolver is unconditionally the throwing stub regardless of where `definitions` lives. **Run-mode dispatch (as opposed to codegen-mode, which has a real "descriptor root") cannot handle `$ref` in ANY composed schema today.**
- **Impact:** any `@adhd/apigen-core-client`-mounted (not code-generated) function whose input/output schema ends up `$ref`-based (which happens automatically whenever a named type is referenced by ≥2+ call sites within one extraction pass — an easy, common case for any real domain model, not an edge case) crashes at dispatch time over BOTH the MCP and HTTP transports. This silently affects every "mount, don't codegen" consumer of `apigen-plugin-mcp`/`apigen-plugin-api-fastify`, not just `@adhd/backlog`.
- **Workaround shipped in `@adhd/backlog`:** `entrypoint/backlog/src/server.ts`'s `dereferenceSchema()` fully inlines every `$ref` (collecting `definitions`/`$defs` from any depth, substituting recursively, dropping the now-unused dicts) BEFORE `composeSchemas()` ever sees the schema — client.ts has no self-referential/recursive types, so this always terminates. This is a workaround, not a fix — the underlying `apigen-engine-runtime`/`apigen-base-logical` limitation remains.
- **Fix direction (upstream, out of `@adhd/backlog`'s scope):** either (a) give `dispatch()`/`RunInput` a way to supply a real `resolve()` override backed by the composed schema's own `definitions`/`$defs` (the "descriptor root" the error message alludes to), or (b) have `composeSchemas()`/the extraction pipeline guarantee every composed schema is fully dereferenced before it ever reaches a plugin, so run-mode `dispatch()` never needs to resolve a `$ref` at all.
- **Status:** OPEN (upstream `@adhd/apigen-engine-runtime`/`@adhd/apigen-base-logical`) — mitigated locally in `@adhd/backlog`.
- Citations: [main, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-implementation, 1: packages/apigen/apigen-base-logical/src/lib/runmode.ts:36-53; 2: packages/apigen/apigen-engine-runtime/src/lib/dispatch.ts:93-102; 3: entrypoint/backlog/src/server.ts (dereferenceSchema); 4: live repro — real MCP `callTool createItem` + real HTTP `POST /backlog/createItem` both threw this exact error before the workaround, both green after]

### BUG-APIGEN-CORE-OUTPUT-DEFS-NOT-HOISTED-001 — `@adhd/apigen-core-client`'s `extract.ts` hoists nested `definitions`/`$defs` for INPUT params only, never for the OUTPUT/return schema

**Status:** MIXED

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-22, same session, diagnosing `BUG-APIGEN-ENGINE-RUNTIME-DISPATCH-REF-UNSUPPORTED-001` above.
- **Where:** `packages/apigen/apigen-core-client/src/lib/extract.ts` — `hoistNestedDefs()` (the documented fix for BUG-APIGEN-029: a nested named type's `definitions`/`$defs` can land at `<fragment>.properties.x.definitions` instead of the fragment's own top level, which a `$ref: "#/definitions/X"` cannot resolve against since resolution is always against the DOCUMENT ROOT) is called once per domain PARAM (`extract.ts:667`, inside the per-param loop) but is never called on `outputSchema` (`extract.ts:679`, `const outputSchema = await buildSchema(...)` — passed straight into the returned `Operation.output` with no hoisting step at all).
- **Impact:** any function whose RETURN type contains a named type resolved via the $ref path can have a dangling nested `definitions` — the exact same class of bug BUG-APIGEN-029 fixed for params, just unfixed for outputs. `composeSchemas()` compounds this: it only merges `input`'s hoisted `definitions`/`$defs` onto the composed input (`compose-schemas.ts` — `inputDefinitions`/`inputDollarDefs`), never `output`'s, so even a well-formed nested-but-nonhoisted output `$ref` has nowhere to be reunited with its `definitions` at the composed-schema level either.
- **Fix direction:** call `hoistNestedDefs()` on `outputSchema` in `extract.ts` (mirroring the param loop), and have `compose-schemas.ts` merge `output`'s hoisted `definitions`/`$defs` onto the composed `output` the same way it already does for `input`. Independent of (and does not fully resolve) `BUG-APIGEN-ENGINE-RUNTIME-DISPATCH-REF-UNSUPPORTED-001` above, which means run-mode dispatch cannot use `$ref` even once correctly hoisted — both need fixing for a `$ref`-based output schema to work end-to-end.
- **Status:** OPEN (upstream `@adhd/apigen-core-client`) — mitigated locally in `@adhd/backlog` via the same `dereferenceSchema()` workaround.
- Citations: [main, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-implementation, 1: packages/apigen/apigen-core-client/src/lib/extract.ts:654-700 (param hoisting) vs :677-714 (output, no hoisting); 2: packages/apigen/apigen-core-client/src/lib/compose-schemas.ts:187-217 (input-only definitions merge); 3: real extracted `createItem.output` for `entrypoint/backlog/dist/client.d.ts` showing a nested un-hoisted `definitions` under `properties.item`]

### DEBT-BACKLOG-CONTENT-HASH-COLLISION-001 — `@adhd/sox-graph-store`'s `writeNode()` dedupes by a GLOBAL, case/whitespace-insensitive content hash, silently colliding two unrelated items with byte-identical (post-normalization) title+body

**Status:** MIXED

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-22, implementing `entrypoint/backlog/src/store/mapping.ts`. `writeNode(content, meta)` in `~/dev/ai/sox-ecosystem/libs/data/graph/graph-store/src/index.ts` dedupes via `sha256(content.trim().toLowerCase())` looked up with NO namespace/repo scoping — a hash hit returns the EXISTING node id and silently discards the new call's `meta` entirely (no error, no merge).
- **Impact:** two different backlog items (different repo, different humanId) whose `title+body` happen to be byte-identical after trim+lowercase would silently collapse into ONE graph node, losing the second item's identity/metadata permanently, with no error surfaced to the caller.
- **Fix direction (workaround shipped):** `entrypoint/backlog/src/store/mapping.ts`'s `buildNodeContent()` appends an HTML-comment uniqueness marker (`<!-- adhd-backlog:{repo}::{humanId} -->`) to every node's `content` before calling `writeNode()` — `humanId` is already globally unique per (repo, family) via the same CAS primitive claims use, so this makes every backlog item's `content` hash-unique by construction, at the cost of negligible FTS noise. Regression-tested in `entrypoint/backlog/src/client.spec.ts` ("two items with byte-identical title+body..."). Upstream, `@adhd/sox-graph-store` could scope its content-hash dedup by `namespace` (or accept an opt-out) to make this a non-issue for every consumer, not just this workaround.
- **Status:** OPEN (upstream `@adhd/sox-graph-store` behavior) — mitigated in `@adhd/backlog`.
- Citations: [main, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-implementation, 1: ~/dev/ai/sox-ecosystem/libs/data/graph/graph-store/src/index.ts writeNode() content-hash dedup + hashContent() trim/lowercase normalization; 2: entrypoint/backlog/src/store/mapping.ts buildNodeContent(); 3: entrypoint/backlog/src/client.spec.ts "two items with byte-identical title+body..."]

### DEBT-BACKLOG-CONTENT-IMMUTABLE-001 — `@adhd/backlog`'s `updateItem` cannot refresh the FTS-indexed `content` after a title/body edit

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-22, implementing `entrypoint/backlog/src/store/crud.ts`. `@adhd/sox-graph-store`'s `touch(nodeId, meta: Partial<NodeMeta>)` covers `name`/`summary`/`topic`/`tags`/`importance`/`confidence`/`tExpires`/`metadata` — never `content` — and there is no other primitive to update a node's `content` after `writeNode()`.
- **Impact:** `updateItem`'s `title`/`body` patch updates `summary` (title) and `metadata.body` (source of truth for every API response) correctly, but the underlying FTS `content` (set once at `createItem` time) does not reflect the edit — `listItems({grep})`/`renderToMarkdown` search may miss a post-edit body term until the item is later superseded (which DOES mint fresh content).
- **Fix direction:** either request an upstream `@adhd/sox-graph-store` content-update primitive, or have `updateItem` internally `supersedeItem` itself on a title/body change (changes public semantics — a superseded id — so not done without a product decision).
- **Status:** OPEN — documented limitation, not blocking v0.1 (title/body edits are correct everywhere except FTS ranking).
- Citations: [main, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-implementation, 1: ~/dev/ai/sox-ecosystem/libs/data/graph/graph-store/src/index.ts touch() — no `content` field; 2: entrypoint/backlog/src/store/crud.ts updateItemNode()]

### DEBT-BACKLOG-AUDIT-TRAIL-PARTIAL-001 — `@adhd/backlog`'s `auditTrail` derives history from durable fields only, not a full transition/claim event log

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-22, implementing `entrypoint/backlog/src/store/query.ts`'s `auditTrail()`. SPEC.md §5.6 documents `AuditTrailEntry.kind` as including `'transition'`/`'claim'`, but the store persists no separate append-only event log — only the LATEST `status`/`claimedBy` and the full `notes`/`citations` arrays.
- **Impact:** `auditTrail()` honestly returns a synthetic `created` entry + every real `note`/`citation` (with citations timestamped by `item.updatedAt` since `Citation` carries no `at` field of its own) + the real `getSupersessionChain()`-derived chain — it does NOT reconstruct a full history of every past status/claim change, since that data was never persisted.
- **Fix direction:** if a full transition/claim history becomes a real requirement, add an explicit append-only event log (e.g. a dedicated `kind:'generic'` node per event, or a `DERIVED_FROM`-linked chain) written by `transitionStatusNode`/`claimItemNode` — a real schema/perf tradeoff not justified for v0.1 given no DoD clause requires it.
- **Status:** OPEN — documented scope reduction, not a bug.
- Citations: [main, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-implementation, 1: entrypoint/backlog/SPEC.md §5.6 AuditTrailEntry; 2: entrypoint/backlog/src/store/query.ts auditTrail()]

### DEBT-BACKLOG-AUTOLINK-DRYRUN-NO-CANDIDATES-001 — `@adhd/sox-analysis`'s `buildAutoLinks` dry-run mode discards its candidate list, returning only `void`

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, authoring `entrypoint/backlog/RAG-SPEC.md` §5.5 (auto-suggested dependency/relation edges with a human/planner confirm gate).
- **Where:** `~/dev/ai/sox-ecosystem/libs/data/analysis/analysis/src/index.ts:1181-1238` — `buildAutoLinks(vec, graph, opts)` computes pairwise cosine-similarity candidates, applies a `maxLinksPerNode` cap, and either calls `graph.writeEdge(...)` per accepted candidate or (when `opts.dryRun` is true) skips the write — but the function's return type is `void` either way. There is no path by which a caller can retrieve the candidate list a dry run would have written.
- **Impact:** any confirm-gate feature (write nothing, show a human/planner the ranked candidates, let them approve individually) cannot be built on top of this function at all, dry-run or not — it is a blind writer or a no-op, with no read-only "compute and return" mode. This blocked `entrypoint/backlog/RAG-SPEC.md` §5.5's `suggestDependencies`/`suggestRelated` design from reusing it.
- **Fix direction (upstream, out of `@adhd/backlog`'s scope):** add a `returnCandidates?: boolean` mode to `AutoLinkOpts` that returns `Array<{a: number; b: number; sim: number}>` instead of `void` when set — mirroring `detectNearDupPairs`' already-correct pure/return-value shape in the same file (`:182-214`), which has no equivalent gap. Until then, any consumer needing a confirm-gated suggestion feature must replicate `buildAutoLinks`'s selection algorithm (pairwise cosine + per-node cap + threshold) in-package rather than calling this function.
- **Workaround adopted in the RAG-SPEC design:** `entrypoint/backlog/RAG-SPEC.md` §5.5's `suggestDependencies`/`suggestRelated` are specified as an in-package, read-only composition directly over `@adhd/sox-vector-store`'s `VectorBackend.knn` and `@adhd/sox-graph-store`'s `getEdges`/`queryNodes`, never calling `buildAutoLinks`. `buildAutoLinks` remains the right choice for the *non-gated* periodic soft-edge sweep (`RAG-SPEC.md` §4/§5.5's `runDedupSweep`), where blind-write-on-threshold is the accepted convention.
- **Status:** OPEN (upstream `@adhd/sox-analysis` behavior) — worked around in `entrypoint/backlog/RAG-SPEC.md`'s design; not blocking, since `@adhd/backlog`'s RAG layer is itself unimplemented (design-only) as of this entry.
- Citations: [main, architect-reviewer, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-rag-design, 1: ~/dev/ai/sox-ecosystem/libs/data/analysis/analysis/src/index.ts:1181-1238 (buildAutoLinks) vs :182-214 (detectNearDupPairs, no equivalent gap); 2: entrypoint/backlog/RAG-SPEC.md §5.5, §9 open question 1]

### FEAT-BACKLOG-RAG-ADOPT-FILTERED-KNN-001 — `@adhd/backlog` must adopt the now-shipped filtered-KNN (`@adhd/sox-vector-store@0.2.0`) to implement its Phase-1 RAG layer

**Status:** MIXED

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, reconciling `FEAT-SOX-VECTOR-STORE-FILTERED-KNN-001` after its Phase-0 (sox-library) half shipped + published, leaving only the adhd-consumer half open. (Supersedes the consumer half of that now-resolved item — see CHANGELOG.)
- **Where:** `@adhd/backlog` currently depends ONLY on `@adhd/sox-graph-store@^0.3.0` (`entrypoint/backlog/package.json:8`) — no `@adhd/sox-vector-store`, `@adhd/sox-embedding-provider`, or `@adhd/sox-hybrid-search`. The RAG layer designed in `entrypoint/backlog/RAG-SPEC.md` (Status: DESIGN ONLY) is unimplemented: nothing in `entrypoint/backlog/src/**` calls `knn`/`nodeFilter`/embeddings.
- **Now unblocked (Phase 0 shipped):** the hard prerequisite is published — `@adhd/sox-vector-store@0.2.0` exposes `VecFilter.nodeFilter` with a `JOIN node n ON n.rowid = v.node_id` + `WHERE buildNodeFilterClause()` pushdown (`vector-store/src/index.ts:25,149-164`), `@adhd/sox-graph-store@0.4.0` exports the formerly-private `buildNodeFilterClause` (`graph-store/src/index.ts:533`), and `@adhd/sox-hybrid-search@0.3.0` already consumes it (`hybrid-search/src/index.ts:488-542`), collapsing the old two-step id-prematerialize workaround and its `SQLITE_LIMIT_VARIABLE_NUMBER` ceiling.
- **Fix direction:** implement RAG-SPEC Phase 1 in `@adhd/backlog` — add `@adhd/sox-vector-store@^0.2.0` (+ an embedding provider, optionally `@adhd/sox-hybrid-search@^0.3.0`) as deps, bump the pinned `@adhd/sox-graph-store` to `^0.4.0`, and wire `semanticSearch`/`relatedItems`/semantic `dedupeScan`/`suggestDependencies` per RAG-SPEC §2–§5 against the scoped `nodeFilter` KNN path. Keep it opt-in (FTS/symbol dedup stays the default), and prove it with a real end-to-end test: a scoped semantic query returns the right item over a real embedded store.
- **Status:** OPEN — Phase 1 (adhd consumer). Phase 0 (sox library) RESOLVED + published; see CHANGELOG. Blocks nothing else; the tool is fully functional today without RAG (semantic dedup/search are the only missing surface).
- Citations: [main, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-rag-reconcile, 1: entrypoint/backlog/package.json:8 (sox-graph-store ^0.3.0, no vector-store dep); 2: ~/dev/ai/sox-ecosystem/libs/data/vectors/vector-store/src/index.ts:25,149-164 (nodeFilter pushdown, shipped 0.2.0); 3: ~/dev/ai/sox-ecosystem/libs/data/graph/graph-store/src/index.ts:533 (buildNodeFilterClause exported, 0.4.0); 4: ~/dev/ai/sox-ecosystem/libs/data/search/hybrid-search/src/index.ts:488-542 (adopts nodeFilter, 0.3.0); 5: entrypoint/backlog/RAG-SPEC.md (Status: DESIGN ONLY, Phase 1)]

### BUG-APIGEN-OPENAPI-ROUTE-PATH-MISMATCH-001 — the `openapi` plugin's advertised paths do not match the `api-fastify` plugin's actually-served routes

**Status:** MIXED

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, driving the live `@adhd/backlog` server (`apiFastifyPlugin.run()` + `openapiPlugin`).
- **Detail:** the served Fastify routes use namespace `backlog` + the camelCase export name — `GET /backlog/getItem`, `POST /backlog/createItem` (confirmed 200). The OpenAPI 3.1 doc served at `GET /_meta/openapi` instead lists `GET /backlog/client-d/get-item`, `POST /backlog/client-d/create-item` — a `client-d` segment (derived from the extraction source basename `client.d.ts`) plus kebab-casing. The two disagree on BOTH the namespace segment and the casing.
- **Evidence:** `GET /backlog/getItem` → 200; `GET /backlog/client-d/get-item` (the OpenAPI-advertised path) → 404. So every path in the generated spec is wrong.
- **Impact:** any client generated from the OpenAPI document (codegen, Swagger UI "try it", a typed SDK) hits 404 on every call — the spec is actively misleading, not merely cosmetic. Cross-plugin contract break between `@adhd/apigen-plugin-openapi` and `@adhd/apigen-plugin-api-fastify`.
- **Fix direction:** the two plugins must derive route path + namespace + casing from ONE shared function (the api-fastify route builder is the source of truth since it's what actually serves) so the OpenAPI `paths` are byte-identical to the registered routes. Likely the openapi plugin re-derives the namespace from `op.path`/the source file rather than reusing the served route string.
- **Status:** OPEN (apigen bug; `@adhd/backlog` works via the real camelCase routes — a `@adhd/backlog` follow-up could pin `--namespace` and pre-extract to avoid the `client-d` basename leak, but the plugin mismatch is the root defect). **MCP side RESOLVED 2026-07-23**: `@adhd/apigen-plugin-mcp` now derives every tool name via `@adhd/apigen-naming`'s `project(op).mcp.name` (`packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-mcp/src/lib/tool-naming.ts`, wired into `run.ts` + `generate.ts`) instead of the raw exported fn name — same shared-authority fix this bug calls for, applied to the MCP transport. **BEHAVIOR CHANGE**: MCP tool names changed (e.g. `getItem` → `<namespace>_<file>_get_item`); any MCP host with a hardcoded old tool name must update. **api-express side RESOLVED 2026-07-23**: `@adhd/apigen-plugin-api-express`'s `run.ts` + `generate.ts` now derive BOTH route path and HTTP verb via `@adhd/apigen-naming`'s `project(op).http` (`packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-api-express/src/lib/route.ts`) instead of hand-rolled `${routePrefix}/${pkg.id}/${fnName}` + the old `httpVerb()` call — `run()` indexes the REAL `Operation[]` off `RunInput.operations` (BUG-APIGEN-024's mechanism) for full multi-segment-path fidelity, proven end-to-end over a live Express server (`route-parity.spec.ts`); `generate()`'s `PluginInput` has no `Operation[]` (see `DEBT-APIGEN-PLUGIN-MCP-GENERATE-OPERATIONS-001`, now confirmed — not just "likely" — to also apply to api-express) so it falls back to a single-path-segment reconstruction from `(pkgId, fnName)`, exact in the common single-source case. **BEHAVIOR CHANGE**: (1) served/generated routes are now kebab-cased per `project()` (e.g. `/pkg/getUser` → `/pkg/get-user`) — any hardcoded client URL must update; (2) the `--opt projection.http.verb.<key>` override key changed from `${pkgId}:${fnName}` to the canonical `Operation.id` slug (`<namespace>/<kebab-path>`), matching openapi/gRPC/CLI's own override key — an existing override config must be re-keyed. **api-fastify side RESOLVED 2026-07-23**: `@adhd/apigen-plugin-api-fastify`'s `run.ts` + `generate.ts` now derive BOTH route path and HTTP verb via `@adhd/apigen-naming`'s `project(op).http` (`packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-api-fastify/src/lib/route-projection.ts`'s `resolveRoute()`/`resolveOperation()`) instead of the hand-rolled `${routePrefix}/${pkg.id}/${fnName}` + the old `httpVerb()` call — `run()` indexes the REAL `Operation[]` off `RunInput.operations` for full multi-segment-path fidelity (proven end-to-end over a live Fastify server against a `backlog/client-d/get-item`-shaped fixture, plus a negative control confirming the old derivation reverts the new assertions to red — `plugin.spec.ts`'s `[BUG-APIGEN-OPENAPI-ROUTE-PATH-MISMATCH-001]` suites); `generate()`'s `PluginInput` has no `Operation[]` (`DEBT-APIGEN-PLUGIN-MCP-GENERATE-OPERATIONS-001`) so it falls back to the same single-path-segment reconstruction pattern as MCP/api-express, exact in the common single-source case. Same **BEHAVIOR CHANGE** as the api-express fix applies (kebab-cased routes; `Operation.id`-keyed verb overrides). **openapi side does not need a code change** — `@adhd/apigen-plugin-openapi` already derives via `project(op).http` (`packages/apigen/codegen/openapi/src/lib/to-openapi.ts`); it was always the OTHER three transports that diverged from it, and with MCP/api-express/api-fastify now all on the shared `project()`/`resolveOperation()`-family pattern, **this bug is RESOLVED across all four transports**.
- **py-flask side RESOLVED 2026-07-23**: `@adhd/apigen-plugin-py-flask` is architecturally different from the other four transports — it never receives a TS-side `Operation[]` at all (`RunInput.operations` is absent for this non-TS-extraction run path; `entrypoint/apigen-cli/src/lib/commands/run.ts` routes `.py` sources straight to `plugin.run()` with an empty `schemas: {}` stub), so there is no TS call site to point at `project()`. The divergence instead lived in the spawned Python server: `apigen_python/flask_server.py` computed its route as `/<ns>/<lastPathSegment.raw>` (dropping every path segment but the last, un-kebab-cased) and its verb from `op.safe` alone (never applying the FEAT-APIGEN-022 primitive-only-input GET-hoist) — both silently diverging from `project()`'s `'/' + [namespace, ...path].map(toKebab).join('/')` + hoist rule. Fixed by reimplementing that SAME algorithm natively in Python (`_route_for_op()` / `_is_primitive_only_input_schema()` / `_http_verb()` in `flask_server.py`) against the identical `{raw, words}` Segment structures `apigen_python.extractor.extract_module()` already produces (documented as mirroring the TS tokenizer) — Python cannot import the TS package, so this is a byte-for-byte re-derivation, not a call into `@adhd/apigen-naming`. `do_GET`/`do_POST` now dispatch via a full-route lookup (`state.route_map`) instead of an `/<ns>/` prefix-strip, which is what makes a multi-segment route (namespace + file-segment + export-segment) resolve at all. Proven end-to-end over a live spawned server (`packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-py-flask/src/test/plugin.spec.ts`'s `[BUG-APIGEN-OPENAPI-ROUTE-PATH-MISMATCH-001]` suite: `project()`-derived routes return 200/verb-correct, the old flat routes now 404, and a negative control confirms all of it goes red against the pre-fix `flask_server.py`, verified by hand). Fix required editing `packages/apigen/python/apigen_python/flask_server.py` — outside the `apigen-plugin-py-flask` package directory but not touched by any of the other four transports' fixes above; this is the shared Python host source the TS plugin spawns, and no TS-side fix was possible given the missing `Operation[]`.
- **`@adhd/backlog` consumer fallout RESOLVED 2026-07-23** (same session, adding the CLI transport): the MCP/api-fastify RESOLVED fixes above (commit `a6e895e2`, landed AFTER `@adhd/backlog`'s own original commit `1be78422`) silently broke `nx test backlog` — `server.spec.ts` asserted the OLD bare route `/backlog/getItem` (now `/backlog/client-d/get-item`, confirmed 404) and `server.mcp.spec.ts` asserted the OLD bare tool name `getItem` (now `backlog_client_d_get_item`, confirmed `MCP error -32603: Unknown tool`) — 4 tests red. Both spec files updated in place to the real canonical names/routes (no plugin-side change needed or made). Confirms the "`@adhd/backlog` follow-up could pin `--namespace` and pre-extract to avoid the `client-d` basename leak" idea above is NOT free even where it would help: HTTP/MCP routes/tool names still leak `client-d` today and remain a real (if now merely cosmetic/OPEN) rough edge for any external HTTP/MCP caller of `@adhd/backlog`. The new THIRD transport added this session (`entrypoint/backlog/src/cli.ts`'s `runBacklogCli`, mounting `@adhd/apigen-plugin-cli-output`) is NOT affected the same way: it derives the real command-table prefix from the live `operations` list at runtime (`resolveCommandPrefix`, never a hardcoded `'backlog'` literal) and transparently prepends it, so a CLI caller types the clean `backlog get-item …` regardless of what the internal file-segment happens to be — the one transport where `@adhd/backlog` fully owns and can hide the caller-facing surface.
- **Status:** RESOLVED 2026-07-23 (all five transports — openapi, mcp, api-express, api-fastify, py-flask — now derive route/verb from the shared `project()` algorithm, either by calling it directly or, for py-flask's Python-side dispatch, by an equivalent re-derivation against the same Segment structures). `DEBT-APIGEN-PLUGIN-MCP-GENERATE-OPERATIONS-001` (the `orchestrateGenerate()`-has-no-`Operation[]` gap the fixed TS transports' `generate()` paths fall back around) remains separately OPEN. Two NEW findings from the py-flask fix are filed separately: `BUG-APIGEN-PYTHON-RUNTESTS-STALE-FIXTURE-PATH-001` (unrelated pre-existing failure in `packages/apigen/python/run_tests.py`) and `DEBT-APIGEN-PYTHON-TEST-CACHE-INPUT-001` (nx cache doesn't invalidate `apigen-plugin-py-flask`/`-py-grpc`'s `test` target on changes to the shared `packages/apigen/python` sources they spawn). `@adhd/backlog`'s own downstream-consumer fallout (stale test assertions) also RESOLVED 2026-07-23 — see bullet above; the `client-d` route/tool-name leak itself remains an OPEN cosmetic rough edge on the HTTP/MCP transports specifically (not a regression, not blocking, tracked here rather than as a new duplicate item).

### BUG-APIGEN-SAFE-OP-MUTATIONS-OVER-GET-001 — the safe-op→GET heuristic routes state-mutating operations as GET

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, same live-server session — enumerating the `@adhd/backlog` GET routes.
- **Detail:** apigen hoists an operation to `GET` (query-param) when its parameters are all scalars, regardless of whether the function mutates state. Of `@adhd/backlog`'s 14 GET routes, only 3 are true reads (`getItem`, `blockers`, `auditTrail`); the other 11 mutate: `addDependency`, `removeDependency`, `linkRelated`, `mergeItems`, `assignItem`, `attachToPlan`, `setPriority`, `appendNote`, `startWork`, `renewClaim`, `softDeleteItem`. A `merge` and a `delete` answering over GET is the sharpest case.
- **Impact:** non-idempotent writes reachable via GET can be triggered by link prefetchers, browser/CDN caches, crawlers, and naive retries — a real correctness/safety hazard, not a style nit. GET responses are also cacheable, so a stale write-result may be served.
- **Fix direction:** the GET-hoist should require BOTH all-scalar params AND an explicit safety signal — honor `x-apigen-safe` (already extracted per-op) / a `@safe`/`@readonly` JSDoc tag / a naming convention, defaulting mutating ops to POST. Interim `@adhd/backlog` mitigation: annotate the 11 mutating client fns so they are not classed safe, once apigen exposes the signal.
- **Status:** OPEN (apigen behavior; affects every apigen-served package, not just backlog).
- Citations: [main, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-serve, 1: live server startup route log — 14 GET incl. mergeItems/softDeleteItem/removeDependency (tmp/backlog/serve.log 2026-07-23); 2: entrypoint/backlog/src/client.ts (the 11 mutating fns with all-scalar params); 3: packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-api-fastify/src/lib/run.ts safe-op GET-hoist]

### BUG-APIGEN-PYTHON-RUNTESTS-STALE-FIXTURE-PATH-001 — `packages/apigen/python/run_tests.py` hardcodes the pre-migration `packages/apigen/plugins/<name>/...` fixture layout; 4 checks currently FAIL

**Status:** MIXED

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, fixing `BUG-APIGEN-OPENAPI-ROUTE-PATH-MISMATCH-001` for py-flask — running `python3 packages/apigen/python/run_tests.py` directly to check for regressions from the `flask_server.py` route/verb fix.
- **Detail:** `run_tests.py` still builds fixture paths as `SCRIPT_DIR.parent / "plugins" / "py-flask" / "src" / "test" / "fixtures" / …` (and the `py-grpc` equivalent) — the PRE-migration layout (`packages/apigen/plugins/<name>/`). The actual current layout (per `@adhd/workspace-codegen-nx` naming, AGENTS.md §1) is `packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-<name>/src/test/fixtures/…`. Every `fixture_module.exists()` guard in the script is therefore `False`, and each corresponding suite short-circuits via `results.fail(...); return` before running any of its real assertions — dead code, not a "gated" skip.
- **Evidence:** `cd packages/apigen/python && python3 run_tests.py` → `EXIT=1`, `Results: 84/88 passed, 4 failed`: `flask.fixture.present`, `cli_decimal.fixture.present`, `future_ann.fixture.present`, `grpc.fixture.present` — all `Fixture module not found: .../packages/apigen/plugins/py-{flask,grpc}/...`. Confirmed pre-existing and unaffected by the `BUG-APIGEN-OPENAPI-ROUTE-PATH-MISMATCH-001` py-flask fix: reverting `flask_server.py` to its pre-fix `HEAD` version and re-running produces the IDENTICAL 4 failures (the script never reaches `flask_server.py`'s code at all — it fails at the path-exists check first).
- **Impact:** the Flask-server live suite (E), the CLI-spawned decimal-decode suite (H), the PEP-563 `from __future__ import annotations` regression suite (J), and the gRPC live suite (I) have not actually executed since the `packages/apigen/plugins/*` → `packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-*` rename — silently, since the runner reports a named `FAIL` rather than crashing, so a `84/88 passed` summary line can look "mostly green" while 4 whole suites (dozens of would-be assertions) never ran. This is the SAME class of regression `BUG-WORKSPACE-GEN-001`/the `AGENTS.md` symlink note warns about for docs — a directory-rename left stale absolute-path references in tooling, not just prose.
- **Fix direction:** update the 4 `SCRIPT_DIR.parent / "plugins" / "<name>" / …` path expressions (`run_tests.py:933,1341,1588,1824`) to `SCRIPT_DIR.parent / "apigen-plugin-<name>" / …`, matching the current package directory names. Given this script is unwired to any Nx target or CI workflow (no `project.json` for `packages/apigen/python`, no `.github/workflows` reference found), also consider whether it should gain an `nx` target (e.g. via `@nxlv/python`) so this class of drift is caught automatically going forward instead of requiring a human/agent to run it by hand.
- **Status:** OPEN — not fixed as part of `BUG-APIGEN-OPENAPI-ROUTE-PATH-MISMATCH-001` (py-flask fix): out of that task's declared scope (`packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-py-flask/` only), and the `py-grpc` fixture path fix would touch the sibling `py-grpc` plugin, which was not part of this fix.
- Citations: [main, claude (sonnet-5), BUG-APIGEN-OPENAPI-ROUTE-PATH-MISMATCH-001 (py-flask), 1: packages/apigen/python/run_tests.py:933,1341,1588,1824 (stale `plugins/<name>` paths); 2: terminal `python3 packages/apigen/python/run_tests.py` → EXIT=1, `Results: 84/88 passed, 4 failed`, all 4 `Fixture module not found` (2026-07-23); 3: confirmed unaffected by flask_server.py fix — identical 4 failures against both pre-fix (`git show HEAD:...`) and post-fix `flask_server.py`]

### DEBT-APIGEN-PYTHON-TEST-CACHE-INPUT-001 — nx caches `apigen-plugin-py-flask`/`-py-grpc`'s `test` target across changes to the shared `packages/apigen/python` sources they spawn at runtime

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, same `BUG-APIGEN-OPENAPI-ROUTE-PATH-MISMATCH-001` py-flask fix session, running the negative-control proof (temporarily reverting `packages/apigen/python/apigen_python/flask_server.py` to `HEAD` to confirm the new parity tests go red).
- **Detail:** `CI=true npx nx run apigen-plugin-py-flask:test` immediately after reverting `flask_server.py` to `HEAD` reported `[local cache]` and **passed** (using the STALE, already-cached pass from before the revert) — the test did not actually re-execute. `packages/apigen/python` is a plain filesystem directory (no `project.json`, not an Nx project, referenced only via `@adhd/apigen-python-env`'s `resolvePythonPkgDir()` relative-path walk), so it is not — and cannot trivially be, without registering it as an Nx project or adding an explicit `inputs` entry — part of `apigen-plugin-py-flask`'s Nx input hash. Only `npx nx reset` (clearing the whole cache) forced a real re-run, which then correctly reported `EXIT=1` with 10/14 tests red.
- **Impact:** any future change to `packages/apigen/python/apigen_python/*.py` (flask_server.py, grpc_server.py, extractor.py, runtime.py, validator.py, errors.py) or `apigen_logical.py` can silently fail to invalidate `apigen-plugin-py-flask:test` / `apigen-plugin-py-grpc:test`'s Nx cache — a real regression in the spawned Python host can ship while `nx test` (and CI, if it relies on the Nx cache rather than `nx reset`ing per run) reports a stale, misleadingly-green pass. This is a live instance of exactly the failure mode AGENTS.md §7 point 4 warns about ("trust exit codes, not stdout") one level up the stack — the exit code itself was correct for the CACHED run, but the cache entry was stale relative to the actual `packages/apigen/python` content on disk.
- **Fix direction:** add `packages/apigen/python/**` as an explicit `inputs` entry on `apigen-plugin-py-flask`'s (and `apigen-plugin-py-grpc`'s) `test` target in `project.json` (Nx supports named/glob file-set inputs independent of the project graph), so a change to the spawned Python sources correctly busts the cache. Longer-term, registering `packages/apigen/python` as a proper Nx project (Python, via `@nxlv/python` per AGENTS.md §1 "Non-JS packages") would let Nx's project-graph dependency inference handle this automatically instead of a hand-maintained glob.
- **Status:** OPEN.

### BUG-APIGEN-LOGICAL-NULL-OBJECT-RESULT-INT64-001 — `apigen-base-logical`'s run-mode transcoder wraps a legitimate `null` result (for an object-typed output schema) in a nonsensical `{"$apigen":"int64","v":"null"}` envelope

**Status:** MIXED

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, adding `@adhd/backlog`'s CLI transport (`entrypoint/backlog/src/cli.ts`) and manually smoke-testing `get-item` against a non-existent id. `client.ts`'s `getItem` returns `BacklogItem | null`, and ts-json-schema-generator (this repo's tsconfig has no `strictNullChecks`) emits the OUTPUT schema as a plain `{type:'object', properties:{...}}` with no `null`/`oneOf` branch at all — the `| null` half of the TS type is silently dropped from the generated schema. Reproduced identically over BOTH the CLI (`backlog get-item --repo … --human-id <missing>`) and a live HTTP `GET /backlog/client-d/get-item?...` for a non-existent item: both return **HTTP 200 / exit 0** with body `{"$apigen":"int64","v":"null"}` instead of a bare JSON `null`.
- **Root cause (traced to exact lines, not guessed):** `packages/apigen/apigen-base-logical/src/lib/runmode.ts`'s `encodeNode()`, the `type:'object'` branch (`runmode.ts:122-124`): `if (value === null || typeof value !== 'object' || Array.isArray(value)) { return encodeSchemaless(value, schema, ctx); }` — a `null` result against an object-typed schema is treated as a "schema-less / any position" value rather than passed through as a literal JSON `null`. `encodeSchemaless()` (`runmode.ts:297-319`) then does `for (const id of ctx.registry.ids()) { try { codec.encode(value, ...) } catch { /* next */ } }` — "first codec whose `encode()` call does not throw, wins" — and the `int64` codec's encoder is `String($)` (`hints.spec.ts:371`, confirmed: `TEMPLATE_CELLS.typescript['int64'].encode === 'String($)'`), which is `String(null) === 'null'` — a valid string, so it NEVER throws for ANY input including `null`. Whichever codec with an unconditionally-non-throwing encoder happens to be registered first (here, `int64`) wins by construction, regardless of whether it has any real relationship to the value.
- **Impact:** any apigen-mounted function whose return type is `T | null` (or any other case where the generated schema's "any position" fallback receives `null`) returns a wrong, self-describing-envelope-wrapped result on every transport (HTTP, MCP, CLI — all three share this one `dispatch()`/transcoder). A consumer expecting a bare `null` (the overwhelmingly common "not found" contract) instead gets `{"$apigen":"int64","v":"null"}` and must special-case it, or worse, silently misinterprets it. Not merely cosmetic: any generic JSON client (including this repo's own `apigen-plugin-openapi`-advertised contract) is actively lied to about the response shape.
- **Fix direction (upstream, out of `@adhd/backlog`'s scope):** two independent, complementary fixes: (a) `encodeNode`'s `type:'object'` branch should pass a literal `null` straight through as `null` (never route it into the schema-less envelope heuristic at all — a missing/absent value is never a codec's responsibility); (b) separately, `encodeSchemaless`'s "first non-throwing codec wins" heuristic is unsound in general (a permissive `String($)`-style encoder will always mask a genuinely-unmatched value) — it should require every candidate codec to also pass a matching *decode-that-round-trips* check, or better, never be reached for `null`/`undefined` at all. No `@adhd/backlog`-side workaround was applied (this only reproduces on a NOT-FOUND result — every DoD test seeds the item first, so it isn't blocking, but `cli.spec.ts` deliberately does not assert the null-return shape, to avoid encoding this bug's wrong shape as expected behavior).
- **Status:** OPEN (upstream `@adhd/apigen-base-logical`) — not mitigated in `@adhd/backlog`; low real-world impact today since every currently-shipped `@adhd/backlog` DoD test only exercises the found-item path, but worth fixing before any consumer relies on a bare `null` not-found contract.
- Citations: [main, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-cli-entrypoint, 1: packages/apigen/apigen-base-logical/src/lib/runmode.ts:122-124 (object-type branch routes `null` to `encodeSchemaless`); 2: packages/apigen/apigen-base-logical/src/lib/runmode.ts:297-319 (`encodeSchemaless`'s first-non-throwing-codec heuristic); 3: packages/apigen/apigen-base-logical/src/lib/hints.spec.ts:371 (`int64` TS encode template is `String($)`, never throws); 4: live repro — `node entrypoint/backlog/dist/index.js get-item --repo acme/demo --human-id NOPE-999` → exit 0, stdout `{"$apigen":"int64","v":"null"}` (2026-07-23); 5: live repro — real HTTP `GET /backlog/client-d/get-item?repo=acme%2Fdemo&humanId=NOPE-1` → 200, identical body, same session; 6: entrypoint/backlog/src/cli.spec.ts (deliberately does not assert the not-found/null shape, to avoid codifying this bug)]

### DEBT-BACKLOG-CLI-EAGER-STORE-OPEN-001 — `runBacklogCli` opens the real backing SQLite store even for a bare `--help`/no-args invocation

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, manually smoke-testing the new `backlog` CLI bin (`entrypoint/backlog/src/cli.ts`) — a first `backlog --help` run from the repo root (before scoping subsequent runs to a temp dir) created a REAL `.adhd/backlog/production/data/backlog.db` directly in this repo's own root, and a separate `--help` invocation with no explicit scope touched the real machine-global `~/.adhd/backlog/production/data/backlog.db`'s mtime. Both were caught and cleaned up (`rm -rf` of the one accidental repo-root `.adhd/backlog/`, confirmed nothing else under `.adhd/` was affected) before this session's commit — never left in the tree.
- **Root cause:** `runBacklogCli` (mirroring `startBacklogServer`'s identical existing structure — not a regression introduced by the CLI addition, an inherited property of the shared `buildBacklogApigenPackage(ctx)` helper both transports call) unconditionally does `buildBacklogEnv(...)` → `env.ensureDirs()` → `openGraphBacklogStore(...)` BEFORE ever inspecting `argv` — so even `backlog --help` / bare `backlog` (which only need the STATIC command table derived from `pkg.schemas`, never `ctx`/the open store) pays the cost of, and side effect of, opening a real SQLite file first.
- **Impact:** low severity (opening a SQLite connection is generally non-destructive), but genuinely surprising CLI UX — a purely informational `--help` invocation from an unexpected working directory can create/touch a real graph-store file the caller never asked for, exactly as it did during this session's own smoke-testing. Also unnecessary latency on the single most common one-off invocation (`--help`).
- **Fix direction:** `runBacklogCli` could special-case `argv.length === 0 || argv[0] === '--help' || argv[0] === '-h'` (mirroring `@adhd/apigen-plugin-cli-output`'s own `run()` top-level short-circuit) and print the usage listing directly from `pkg.schemas`/`operations` WITHOUT ever opening the store — `buildBacklogApigenPackage` only needs a `ctx` object at all because its returned `pkg.createClient` closure captures it, which is irrelevant for a `--help` dispatch that never reaches `createClient`. Not fixed in this session (scope: add the CLI transport, not redesign its startup ordering) — filed for a follow-up.
- **Status:** OPEN — low severity, cosmetic/UX, not blocking. Shared with `startBacklogServer` (HTTP/MCP transports have the identical eager-open property, just less visible since a long-lived server's own startup log always shows the DB path).
- Citations: [main, claude (sonnet-5), backlog-cli-entrypoint, 1: entrypoint/backlog/src/cli.ts `runBacklogCli` (env→store→ctx built before argv is ever inspected); 2: entrypoint/backlog/src/server.ts `startBacklogServer` (identical pre-existing ordering, not introduced by the CLI transport); 3: live repro — accidental `.adhd/backlog/production/data/backlog.db` creation at the real repo root from an un-scoped `backlog --help` run, caught and removed same session]

### FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-000 — EPIC: transport-neutral serve-core + thin adapters (umbrella)

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, architecture review of `docs/apigen/proposals/transport-serve-core-refactor.md` (v0.2) against the real code.
- **Detail:** Every apigen transport plugin (`api-fastify`, `api-express`, `mcp`, `cli-output`, `py-flask`) re-implements the op→wire serve loop instead of delegating to one authority, causing the same class of drift to recur (route/verb/envelope derivation duplicated 4 TS ways + 1 Python reimplementation; `--use` mount metadata dropped; MCP missing validate-layer entirely; SPEC §11 streaming built but unwired). Full analysis, corrected architecture, and revision rationale: `docs/apigen/proposals/transport-serve-core-refactor.md`. Child work, in phase-graph order:
  1. `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-005` — Phase 1: `OpPlan` + serve-core primitives + `TransportAdapter` port + `api-fastify` reference migration (also closes `DEBT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-004` mount-fidelity and half of `DEBT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-002` streaming-wiring, for fastify).
  2. `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-006` — Phase 2a: `api-express` migration (closes `DEBT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-003`). Depends on (1).
  3. `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-007` — Phase 2b: `mcp` migration (closes `BUG-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-001`, the other half of `DEBT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-002`). Depends on (1).
  4. `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-008` — Phase 2c: `cli-output` migration. Depends on (1).
  5. `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-009` — Phase 3: py-flask/py-grpc extract/serve split; deletes the Python `project()` port. Depends on (1); independent of (2)-(4).
  6. `DEBT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-011` — Phase 4 (`--source` as source-plugin) — **PARKED**, not scheduled; see its own entry for the revisit condition.
  - (2), (3), (4) are mutually independent and may run in parallel once (1) lands. (5) only depends on (1)'s `OpPlan` shape being stable, not on (2)-(4).
  - **Explicitly out of scope / not fixed by this initiative** (do not close via this epic): `BUG-APIGEN-SAFE-OP-MUTATIONS-OVER-GET-001` (a correctness defect in `project()`'s GET-hoist rule itself, `packages/apigen/apigen-engine-naming/src/lib/naming.ts:150-151` — centralizing consumption of the rule doesn't fix the rule; the parity gate in each child item's acceptance criteria must include a case that proves this bug's behavior is unchanged, not silently patched over) and `BUG-APIGEN-CLI-SERVE-FRONT-PROXY-DOUBLE-SEGMENT-001` (`entrypoint/apigen-cli/src/lib/commands/serve.ts`, outside this scope, though it becomes trivially fixable once `plan.http.route` is the one source of truth).
- **Fix direction:** execute child items in the dependency order above; each child item's own acceptance criteria is its parity gate (per the proposal doc §6). Do not mark this epic done until all of (1)-(5) are CHANGELOG'd; (6) stays open/parked indefinitely pending its revisit trigger.
- **Status:** OPEN.

### FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-005 — Phase 1: `OpPlan` + serve-core primitives (`createPackageInvoker`/`dispatchForPlan`) + `TransportAdapter` port; migrate `api-fastify` as the reference adapter

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, architecture review of the serve-core-refactor proposal against `apigen-engine-runtime`/`apigen-plugin-api-fastify`.
- **Detail:** **Scope (files):** new — `packages/apigen/apigen-engine-runtime/src/lib/{op-plan,package-invoker,dispatch-for-plan}.ts` (or equivalent), exported from `apigen-engine-runtime/src/index.ts`; a new `TransportAdapter` type in `apigen-engine-naming` or `apigen-engine-runtime`. Modified — `packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-api-fastify/src/lib/{run.ts,generate.ts,route-projection.ts,stream.ts}` rewritten as a `TransportAdapter` implementation consuming `OpPlan`; `route-projection.ts`'s `resolveRoute`/`resolveOperation` logic moves into `OpPlan` construction and is deleted from the plugin. Must also: (a) absorb the byte-identical `UsePlugin`/`readUsePlugins`/`readUseOptions`/`adaptCoreLayer`/`buildInvokerForPackage` block (`apigen-plugin-api-fastify/src/lib/run.ts:91-166`, verified line-for-line identical to `apigen-plugin-api-express/src/lib/run.ts:84-159`) into `createPackageInvoker`; (b) fix the `--use` mount-metadata loss (`MountRoute{route,handler}` at `run.ts:172-175`, `collectMountRoutes` hardcoding `{method:'GET',text:'',params:[]}` at `run.ts:189-210,378-392` — this is `DEBT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-004`, folded in here rather than filed separately since Phase 1 deletes this exact code) so mount ops carry full `kind`/`safe`/`input`/`text` fidelity through `OpPlan` and (decision required, proposal §8.1) flow through `--use` layer composition like source ops; (c) wire the existing-but-dead `sendStreamSse` (`apigen-plugin-api-fastify/src/lib/stream.ts:76-148`, confirmed via grep to have zero call sites outside its own module/dist/spec) live for `streaming:true` ops — this is half of `DEBT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-002` (the other half, MCP, is `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-007`). Build the shared golden-fixture parity harness here (proposal §6) since this is the first phase to need it; later phases extend it.
  **Acceptance criteria (parity gate):** per proposal §6 — (1) golden capture of the CURRENT live fastify server (route/verb table, response bytes, error status/code, mount-route output, envelope round-trip) for a fixture set covering safe/scalar, unsafe/mutating-scalar (the `BUG-APIGEN-SAFE-OP-MUTATIONS-OVER-GET-001` shape — included to PROVE this refactor doesn't silently change its behavior), session-envelope, `streaming:true`, and a `--use` mount op; (2) post-migration re-capture against the adapter-based server asserts deep-equality against the committed snapshot; (3) mandatory negative control — revert the migration (or introduce a deliberate one-line regression) and confirm the parity suite goes RED, recorded in the commit/PR; (4) extends `apigen-plugin-api-fastify/src/test/plugin.spec.ts` (already does real-server-driven route/verb parity per `BACKLOG.md:1173-1181`'s citation of `plugin.spec.ts:768-1013`).
  **Dependencies:** none (first phase).
  **Effort:** Large — new runtime module + full rewrite of one transport plugin + first-time build of the parity-gate harness.
  **Architect review folds (2026-07-23, GO-WITH-CHANGES — mandatory in Phase-1 scope):** (F1) `dispatchForPlan` returns `Promise<LayerResult>` and `TransportAdapter.registerRoute`'s dispatch callback / `writeResult` use the REAL `LayerResult` (`invoke()` always returns a Promise; only the resolved value is the `unknown|AsyncIterable` union) — the proposal §3b/§3c bare-union sketch does not type-check. (F2) `OpPlan.cliFlags` value MUST carry `envVar` (mirrors cli-output `FlagSpec`) or Phase-2c silently regresses `parseArgs`'s flag→env-var fallback. (F3) `dispatchForPlan`'s mount branch adapts runtime `Call`→core-client `Call` and needs a `Transport` to stamp: resolve in Phase 1 via an `OpPlan.transport` field stamped per-package (a hardcoded `'http'` would mis-tag every non-HTTP transport's mount provenance once mcp/cli land) — explicit Phase-1 DoD, not an implicit TODO. (Topology) `UsePlugin`/`readUsePlugins`/`readUseOptions`/`adaptCoreLayer`/`buildInvokerForPackage` are PROMOTED into `createPackageInvoker` and DELETED from fastify's file (express then collapses onto it) — name the migration + deletion so Phase-2 express can't keep a divergent copy. GO §8.1 (mount ops through the composed invoker — closes the `--use auth` layer bypassing mount routes at `fastify run.ts:380-391`) and GO §8.2 (wire `sendStreamSse`/`projectStreamMcp` live — a `streaming:true` op today is `JSON.stringify`'d to garbage) are both baked as tested Phase-1/2 DoD clauses. Folded citations: [main, apigen-serve-core-planbuilder, claude (opus), apigen-serve-core, 8: packages/apigen/apigen-engine-runtime/src/lib/invoke.ts:94-102,152-156 (LayerResult/InvokeFn — F1); 9: packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-cli-output/src/lib/run.ts:53-59,300-306 (FlagSpec/env-var fallback — F2); 10: packages/apigen/apigen-core-client/src/lib/plugin.ts:87-117 (core-client Call.transport — F3); 11: packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-api-fastify/src/lib/run.ts:91-166,380-391 (promoted block + mount bypass); 12: docs/plan/apigen-serve-core/ (state machine plan — serve-core-primitives/fastify-adapter states, interfaces.json, dod.12/13/14)]
- **Fix direction:** implement `OpPlan`/`createPackageInvoker`/`dispatchForPlan`/`TransportAdapter` per proposal §3a-§3c; migrate `api-fastify` per proposal §5 Phase 1.
- **Status:** OPEN.

### BUG-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-001 — `apigen-plugin-mcp`'s `run.ts` never composes `createInvoker`/`makeValidateLayer`; malformed MCP tool input reaches the domain function unvalidated

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, architecture review of the serve-core-refactor proposal — reading `apigen-plugin-mcp/src/lib/run.ts` against `apigen-plugin-api-fastify`/`-api-express`/`-cli-output`'s `run.ts`.
- **Detail:** Every other transport's `run.ts` composes `createInvoker([...useLayers, makeValidateLayer(schemas)])` and validates `domainArgs`/`envelope` against the composed input schema BEFORE dispatch (SPEC §6/§8.1 rule 1) — `apigen-plugin-api-fastify/src/lib/run.ts:298`, `apigen-plugin-api-express/src/lib/run.ts:244`, `apigen-plugin-cli-output/src/lib/run.ts:394`. `apigen-plugin-mcp/src/lib/run.ts` does not: its `CallToolRequestSchema` handler (`run.ts:126-173`) calls `dispatch()` directly (`run.ts:143-154`) — `createInvoker`/`makeValidateLayer` do not appear anywhere in the file's imports (`run.ts:1-22`). Two consequences: (1) a schema-violating MCP tool call reaches the target function unvalidated, unlike every other transport; (2) no `--use` layer plugin (auth, logging, rate-limiting) can ever wrap an MCP call — `readUsePlugins`/`readUseOptions`/mount support don't exist in this file at all, so MCP also cannot host `--use` mount ops today (a capability gap, not just a metadata-fidelity gap like fastify/express's `DEBT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-004`). Separately, `run.ts:269-276` rebuilds the ENTIRE tool table (`buildMcpServer`, including all `toolMetas` derivation) on EVERY request in `streaming-http` transport mode, rather than once at startup — a latent perf issue that Phase 1's "computed once" `OpPlan` principle naturally fixes as a side effect when applied here.
  **Scope (files):** `packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-mcp/src/lib/{run.ts,generate.ts}` migrated onto the `TransportAdapter` port from `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-005`, wiring `createPackageInvoker` (validate-layer + `--use` layer/mount composition) for the first time, and wiring `projectStreamMcp`/`projectStreamMcpFull` (`apigen-plugin-mcp/src/lib/stream.ts:77-165`, also confirmed unwired via grep — the other half of `DEBT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-002`) live for `streaming:true` ops. Also hoist `toolMetas` computation out of the per-request `streaming-http` handler.
  **Acceptance criteria (parity gate):** golden-capture/re-capture/negative-control per the Phase-1 harness, using a REAL `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` client (per `AGENTS.md` §"Proving an MCP server works" — never call `run.ts` internals) against the current live MCP server, for the same fixture classes as Phase 1 PLUS an explicit malformed-input case that today succeeds (wrongly) and must reject with `invalid_argument` after this fix — flagged in the PR/commit as an intentional correctness fix presenting as a breaking behavior change, not silent.
  **Dependencies:** `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-005`.
  **Effort:** Large — MCP is gaining invoker composition and streaming support for the first time, not just collapsing onto an existing shape; higher risk than Phase 2a/2c.
  **MCP SSE / streaming-HTTP transport fold (2026-07-24, folded into FEAT-007's scope):** the mcp migration must also preserve AND harden the HTTP-serving transport paths, which are currently UNTESTED (no SSE/StreamableHTTP driving test exists in `apigen-plugin-mcp/src/test/`). Concrete issues: (1) **`BUG-APIGEN-MCP-STREAMING-HTTP-NO-ERROR-GUARD-001` (real, previously unfiled):** the `streaming-http` request handler (`run.ts:269-276`) has NO try/catch, unlike the `sse` handler which explicitly wraps its body (`run.ts:243-249`) precisely so "a handler rejection [does not] become an unhandled rejection that tears down the whole server." A rejection in `reqServer.connect()`/`mcpTransport.handleRequest()` is therefore unhandled and can crash the process — the exact class the SSE path guards against. Fix: give streaming-http the same guard (route error mapping through the adapter's `writeError` under the serve-core rewrite). (2) **SSE lifecycle must survive the TransportAdapter rewrite:** GET `/sse` handshake (`SSEServerTransport`, per-connection), POST `/messages` session routing by `sessionId`, `onclose` session-map cleanup, and the "do NOT call `start()` twice or the SDK throws and crashes the process" invariant (`run.ts:213-262`) must be preserved and pinned by tests. (3) the per-request FULL-server rebuild in streaming-http (`buildMcpServer(input, logger)` at `run.ts:270`) wastefully rebuilds the tool table per request — the legitimately-per-request piece is only the stateless `StreamableHTTPServerTransport` instance; the tool table/`toolMetas` hoist is dod.12. Parity gate MUST add a real SSE client + real StreamableHTTP client exercising handshake, session routing, graceful-error-without-teardown (a second request still succeeds after a handler error), and abort/shutdown, with a negative control (dod.15). Folded citations: [main, apigen-serve-core-planbuilder, claude (opus), apigen-serve-core, 6: packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-mcp/src/lib/run.ts:212-288 (sse+streaming-http serving loop; 269-276 missing error guard vs 243-249 present); 7: packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-mcp/src/test/ (no SSE/StreamableHTTP transport test today); 8: docs/plan/apigen-serve-core/ (mcp-adapter state + dod.15)]
- **Fix direction:** treat as part of the Phase 2 mcp migration (`FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-007` — tracked jointly with this bug since the fix is the migration itself, not a separate patch that would be immediately superseded).
- **Status:** OPEN.
- Citations: [main, architect-reviewer, claude (sonnet-5), apigen-serve-core-refactor, 1: packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-mcp/src/lib/run.ts:1-22,126-173,143-154,178-288,265-276; 2: packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-mcp/src/lib/stream.ts:77-165 (unwired, confirmed via grep for call sites outside dist/spec); 3: packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-api-fastify/src/lib/run.ts:153-166,298 (contrast — validate-layer composition present); 4: packages/apigen/apigen-engine-runtime/src/lib/validate-layer.ts:225-257; 5: docs/apigen/proposals/transport-serve-core-refactor.md §1,§5]

### FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-008 — Phase 2c: migrate `cli-output` onto the shared `TransportAdapter`

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, same review — `apigen-plugin-cli-output/src/lib/run.ts` read in full against the proposal's `OpPlan` sketch.
- **Detail:** **Scope (files):** `packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-cli-output/src/lib/{run.ts,generate.ts,schema-introspect.ts}`. CLI's request-read side is structurally different from HTTP/MCP — an argv flag table (`buildFlagTable`/`parseArgs`, `run.ts:195-315`: kebab flag names, `--no-` boolean negation, JSON-typed flags, envelope env-var fallback via `envelopeCliFlag`/`envelopeEnvVar`), not query/body/tool-args — so this migration needs `OpPlan.cliFlags` (proposal §3a) computed once from the schema, with the adapter's `readCall` reduced to pure argv-walking against the precomputed table. `cli-output` currently has NO `--use` layer/mount support at all (`readUsePlugins`/`usePlugins` never referenced in `run.ts`) — decide during this migration whether to add it (consistent with fastify/express/mcp after Phase 1/2b) or explicitly declare CLI `--use`-incapable and file that as its own follow-up; do not leave it an unstated gap.
  **Acceptance criteria (parity gate):** golden-capture via a REAL spawned child process (argv in, stdout/exit-code out — matches `apigen-plugin-cli-output/src/test/run-cli-integration.spec.ts`'s existing pattern) for the standard fixture set plus a flag-parsing edge case (`--no-` negation, JSON-typed flag, env-var-fallback envelope field) and the usage/help-text path (`formatUsage`, `run.ts:169-177`). Negative control mandatory per the shared harness.
  **Dependencies:** `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-005`.
  **Effort:** Medium — narrower surface (one-shot dispatch, no long-lived server) but the argv-binding logic is genuinely new modeling work, not a collapse onto an existing shape.
- **Fix direction:** per proposal §5 Phase 2; implement `OpPlan.cliFlags` per proposal §3a before/alongside this migration.
- **Status:** OPEN.

### FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-009 — Phase 3: py-flask/py-grpc extract/serve split; inject the TS-computed plan; delete the Python `project()` port

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, architecture review — `apigen-plugin-py-flask/src/lib/plugin.ts` and `packages/apigen/python/apigen_python/flask_server.py` read in full against the proposal's Phase-3 claim.
- **Detail:** **Corrects a proposal-v0.1 framing error** (see `docs/apigen/proposals/transport-serve-core-refactor.md` §9 revision notes): "inject the plan into py-flask as data" is not a small change. Today the TS `py-flask` plugin (`apigen-plugin-py-flask/src/lib/plugin.ts:108-182`) never extracts Python operations at all — it spawns `python3 -m apigen_python.flask_server --module <path> --namespace <ns>` and the Python process does its OWN independent extraction (`flask_server.py:572-580`, `extract_module()`). There is no existing TS-side `Operation[]` and no existing channel to inject a plan through. `flask_server.py:247-314` (`_route_for_op`/`_is_primitive_only_input_schema`/`_http_verb`) is consequently a hand-maintained, byte-for-byte re-derivation of `project()`'s algorithm (confirmed identical logic, different language) against the same `{raw, words}` Segment JSON shape the TS extractor produces.
  **Scope (files):** new — a `--emit-json` extract-only CLI mode on `apigen_python.extractor` (or a new thin wrapper module) that runs `extract_module()` and prints `Operation[]` JSON without starting a server; modified — `apigen-plugin-py-flask/src/lib/plugin.ts`'s `run()` gains a first subprocess call (extract-only) whose stdout is parsed and projected via the REAL `project()` (`apigen-engine-naming/src/lib/naming.ts:143-177`) before spawning the real server; modified — `flask_server.py`'s `_ServerState`/`_build_state` (`flask_server.py:332-356,572-611`) accepts a `--plan <path>` argument carrying precomputed `{op, route, verb}` triples and builds `route_map` directly from it; deleted — `flask_server.py:247-314`'s three re-derivation functions, entirely. Same two-phase pattern applies to `apigen-plugin-py-grpc` (not read in this review — verify its `run.ts`/spawned Python server shape independently before assuming identical scope).
  **Acceptance criteria (parity gate):** golden-capture/re-capture/negative-control against a REAL spawned Python HTTP server (per the pattern `apigen-plugin-py-flask/src/test/plugin.spec.ts` already uses, cited in `BACKLOG.md:1173-1181` for the route-mismatch fix's own negative control) — MUST re-run even though Phase 1's HTTP-side work is long done by this point, since the extract/serve split is a second, independent change to the same server that could reintroduce drift on its own.
  **Dependencies:** `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-005` (needs `OpPlan`'s shape to be stable so the serialized plan format doesn't change twice). Independent of Phase 2 (2a/2b/2c).
  **Open question carried from the proposal (§8.3):** whether `apigen_python.extractor.extract_module()` is safe to invoke as a separate process from `flask_server.py`'s own invocation of it (no import-time side effects tied to the specific process that later serves) was NOT verified in this review (`apigen_python/extractor.py` was not read) — verify before starting this item.
  **Effort:** Large — new IPC/two-phase protocol across a language boundary, plus deletion of hand-audited parity-critical Python code.
  **Architect review fold (2026-07-23, F4 — CONFIRMED divergent, resize; mandatory in FEAT-009 scope):** py-grpc is NOT "same pattern as py-flask, cheaper." Its Python-side naming implements a DIFFERENT algorithm from `project()`'s gRPC rule — single-segment `namespace.capitalize()+"Service"` + raw fn-name method dispatch (`grpc_server.py:397-421,448-459`) — whereas py-flask at least re-derives the SAME HTTP algorithm. So the extract/serve split must ADDITIONALLY RECONCILE py-grpc's service/method naming to `project()`'s real grpc projection (`naming.ts:165-166`): the injected TS plan carries `grpc.package/service/method` and `grpc_server` builds its dispatch table from THAT, deleting the divergent naming. Resize this from a verify-then-proceed checkbox to its own non-trivial reconciliation. Separately, py-grpc rejecting `streaming:true` ops for this epic is a DOCUMENTED, filed deferral (gRPC natively streams; a consumer will want it — a scope boundary, not a permanent capability verdict), tracked as DEFER-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-PYGRPC-STREAMING-001 in the plan BACKLOG. Folded citations: [main, apigen-serve-core-planbuilder, claude (opus), apigen-serve-core, 5: packages/apigen/python/apigen_python/grpc_server.py:397-421,448-459 (divergent grpc naming — F4); 6: packages/apigen/apigen-engine-naming/src/lib/naming.ts:165-166 (real grpc projection to reconcile to); 7: docs/plan/apigen-serve-core/contexts/py-grpc-serve-split.md (resized scope + criterion py-grpc-serve-split.5)]
- **Fix direction:** per proposal §3d (corrected) and §5 Phase 3.
- **Status:** OPEN.
- Citations: [main, architect-reviewer, claude (sonnet-5), apigen-serve-core-refactor, 1: packages/apigen/apigen-plugin-py-flask/src/lib/plugin.ts:15-26,108-182; 2: packages/apigen/python/apigen_python/flask_server.py:1-54,247-314,332-356,572-611; 3: packages/apigen/apigen-engine-naming/src/lib/naming.ts:143-177; 4: docs/apigen/proposals/transport-serve-core-refactor.md §3d,§5,§8.3]

### DEBT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-011 — Phase 4 (PARKED): model `--source` as a built-in ts-extraction source-plugin

**Status:** OPEN

- **Discovered:** 2026-07-23, architecture review of the serve-core-refactor proposal's Phase 4 bullet.
- **Detail:** v0.1 of the proposal floated modeling `--source X` as sugar for a built-in `SourcePlugin`, unifying it with `--use` mount plugins under one op-discovery model. Architecture review verdict (proposal §5.1): **park, do not schedule.** No forcing function exists — there is exactly one TS source kind today, and `MountCapability` (`apigen-core-client/src/lib/plugin.ts:332-347`) already works as a separate, orthogonal mechanism without needing this abstraction. It does not touch the bug class the rest of this initiative fixes (op→wire projection, not op discovery). It does not even unify py-flask's asymmetry (`FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-009` is the real fix for that, via the extract/serve split, independent of this). Cost is real and non-trivial (touches `entrypoint/apigen-cli/src/lib/orchestrator.ts`'s descriptor-building path, which every other phase also depends on) for a speculative, currently-unneeded generalization.
  **Scope (files):** N/A while parked — would touch `entrypoint/apigen-cli/src/lib/orchestrator.ts` + a new `SourcePlugin` capability on `apigen-core-client/src/lib/plugin.ts` if ever picked up.
  **Dependencies:** would depend on `FEAT-APIGEN-SERVE-CORE-005` (OpPlan) if ever scheduled; currently blocked on nothing because it is not scheduled.
  **Effort:** Unestimated — deliberately not sized while parked.
- **Fix direction:** do not implement. **Revisit condition** (record here, do not lose): re-open only if/when a second real TS-side source kind (e.g. a proto-file source, a decorator-annotation source, or promoting `--use` mount ops to pseudo-sources for `apigen generate`) is proposed with a concrete consumer — scope it then, against that consumer's actual requirements, not speculatively now.
- **Status:** OPEN — PARKED (not scheduled; see revisit condition above).
